# Changelog

All notable changes to this project are documented in this file.

The format is based on [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/),
and this project adheres to [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/spec/v2.0.0.html).

## Categories used

- **Added** — new features.
- **Changed** — changes to existing functionality.
- **Fixed** — bug fixes.
- **Removed** — features deleted in this release.
- **Deprecated** — features still present but slated for removal.
- **Security** — vulnerability fixes (link to advisory once published).

## Versioning notes

- **Major (`X.0.0`)** — breaking change to a public API contract (any
  `/api/*` route response shape, env-var name change, removal of a
  user-facing page).
- **Minor (`x.Y.0`)** — new feature or non-breaking enhancement.
- **Patch (`x.y.Z`)** — bug fix, security patch, or internal refactor with
  no user-facing change.

The deployed site at [ma.codes](https://ma.codes) tracks `main`, so every
merge to `main` is effectively a deploy. Versions are tagged on `main` at
the maintainer's discretion to mark coherent release boundaries.

---

replaced by a hero-composed 2560×1440 lantern causeway generated against a measured negative-space contract, brought to life by a procedural-only living layer (no ambient video on the site's LCP-critical entry route), given an arrival moment in which the causeway **lights itself away from the viewer** toward the vanishing point, a pointer-only camera drift, and an art-directed scrim whose middle band is deliberately the lightest so the vanishing mist rim-lights the floating laptop — with the four gates the two canvas layers on `/projects` had been missing (`visibilitychange` among still), and the hero chrome reharmonised against the new plate, where measurement showed the ripple rings running at double the saturation of the scene's own firelight and the laptop's hover response was a 2px halo on a 490px element — then stilled and re-tuned in an owner-directed pass: the pointer-linked camera drift removed outright, the causeway's lantern posts handed back to the still plate (the water mask had been treating posts standing IN the lake as lake, so the model redrew them every frame at up to 58 temporal std while the stone held at 0.00), and the ripple rings plus the laptop's hover bloom moved onto the headline's own `#ff6d05` so the hero's chrome and its name read as one light source — and then the scene rebuilt again as a **WebGL resample of the plate** after the procedural water was reported as unconvincing: the row-shear tier withdrawn (its only clearly visible output was a compositing bug — painted rows landing at 0.99x plate against 0.90x for the rows it skipped, in bands that travelled with the wave), replaced by a shader that ripples the lake on the axis that actually carries the effect, shades it by facet tilt (displacement alone moved a smooth dark lake by a mean of 0.77/255), and spreads true perspective drizzle rings across it; then the procedural water rejected outright and the lake **filmed** instead — the withdrawn ambient loop restored on a re-derived verdict, re-masked from the rig's own geometry and cut further by the ghost map itself; the lantern flames moved by warping each lantern's own photographed flame rather than drawing over it, for none of the 1.8-5 MB /projects spends on the same effect; and the hero **reharmonised against the photograph** — a scrim stage well and edge vignette, a chroma-only baked plate grade, a real dark substrate under the nav ring's glyphs, and a contact shadow that grounds the laptop; and finally the causeway's lanterns **set alight with filmed fire** — the ambient loop's mask, which stopped at the lake, extended with a window around each of the eleven flames, so the page shows fire that burns (17.3 temporal std) instead of a warp of a frozen photograph (0.02), for +21 KB rather than the second megabyte the shader existed to avoid — the model's 51px lantern walk removed by re-registering each flame onto the wick the plate photographed, each flame closed on its own loop length against a fade budget derived from the clip's own motion, and the plate's own flames re-baked from the loop's first frame so the still-to-video swap lands under one frame of the fire's own movement; and the hero **re-framed for portrait phones**, where the same scene had been reading 51% brighter and half as deep — traced not to any mobile rendering path but to the aspect ratio itself (the same phone in LANDSCAPE measures within 2.8% of desktop), because `object-fit: cover` on a 16:9 plate shows a portrait viewport only the brightest central 25% of the photograph and crops away every dark framing element, so the backdrop layers now become a 65% band on portrait — which re-frames plate, video, glow rig and ignite together through the one box they share — carry a tone curve to restore the dynamic range a multiply mathematically cannot, and sit in a `100svh` shell so the bottom of the composition is no longer below the iOS toolbar — after which that band's `sizes` declaration was brought into line with it, so a portrait phone stops buying a srcset rung sized for the full-height box the plate no longer paints, and the hero's ripple rings were given back their presence at the two ends of the viewport range where measurement showed them reading as empty space — then given a third dial that lifts only their OUTER edge on phones, on the finding that the boundary the eye actually reads a ring by is the faintest stop in the whole gradient, with the value chosen as the largest one whose radial falloff stays strictly monotonic rather than the largest that looked bright; and the fluid-scale system's **iOS collapse** fixed — WebKit resolves a viewport unit passed straight into `atan2()` wrongly, which overshot `--fluid-max` and pinned `--fluid-scale` to its MAXIMUM (1.3, the widest-desktop scale) on phones instead of the 0.6 floor, rendering all four opted-in pages 2.17× oversized and overflowing them far enough sideways that iOS shrink-to-fit widened the visual viewport the homepage nav reads to pick its layout — repaired by registering the viewport unit as an `@property` `<length>` so it is computed to absolute px before the trig sees it, leaving all 56 scoped rules and 116 `fluid()` call sites untouched; and the `/projects` workshop scene's table candles given a little genuine life — the delivered loop's own flames measuring a temporal std of 1.9/255 against 0.2 at a no-flame control, sub-perceptual under the scrim, so each table flame's own filmed pixels are warped a touch per frame (scaled about the wick, leaning a whisper with height, brightness riding its body), at an amplitude deliberately an order gentler than a first attempt that was rejected as unprofessional: ~1.5px of travel rather than 14, lifting flame temporal std 1.88 -> 3.57 while leaving every hanging fixture bit-identical and the seamless loop seamless; and the `/projects` category filter demoted from a permanent `localStorage` preference to a one-hop session handoff, so opening the page shows the whole portfolio again instead of whichever tab a single past card click happened to pin; and the inter-page transition rebuilt as the **Ember Passage**, replacing the "Stone Passage" — the small near-black slab and its two-baked-image crossfade retired for a full-viewport swarm of ~90,000 GPU embers that lift off the page (nearest the point CLICKED first), flow through a warm-cored darkness, converge onto the MA monogram and hold it as a living constellation of firelight before blasting outward to reveal the destination — for no new asset bytes at all, on a sequence tightened from 2.1 s to ~1.6 s — and that passage **welded to the homepage headline's own colour**, with the shine and glow it asked for bought where they cannot cost hue: a two-lobe ember sprite with a glinting specular pinpoint and a bloom pool carried by the veil pass already being drawn, and — after a per-route destination palette was tried and rejected, then the mark measured rendering YELLOW and then too DEEP — the renderer itself split so the swarm accumulates BRIGHTNESS and the ink is multiplied through once at the end, which is what makes "the monogram is exactly the `#ff6d05` that MUHAMMAD ABDULLAH is written in" a property of the pipeline rather than a tuning; and the footer's guitar-string wordmark stopped coming back mis-shapen — plucked strings were being frozen mid-oscillation whenever the footer scrolled out of view, because the loop that erases a wave was cancelled without ever being restarted; and the two live-data endpoints behind the header and the Now Playing widget repaired after both were reported failing in the same terminal session — the maintenance header's Projects v2 read had outgrown its own 5 s timeout (eleven boards in one aliased query measuring 4.8–8.7 s, so the board signal was lost on most refreshes while the rate-limit line in the same log showed a perfectly healthy budget, the cost of that query being 1 point), now split into per-board paginated queries that also close a silent single-page truncation past 100 items, with the portfolio query's own too-thin headroom re-sized from measurement rather than left to tip over next; and `/api/spotify` returning 502 on every poll for as long as a rejected access token stayed cached — with a bare `catch { }` that made the status code the only evidence there was — now able to refresh and replay once on a 401, and to say what failed; and the Node-version guard extended from `dev` to the rest of the toolchain — `engine-strict` only enforces `engines.node` at install time and `dev.mjs` covered only the dev server, leaving `npm run build` (the command that leans hardest on the very webpack cache serialization the Node 25 V8 bug corrupts) running on whatever Node invoked npm, which for anything outside an interactive shell is Homebrew's v25; and the homepage's arrival ignite — the light that runs the causeway away from been top-anchored by an in-flow hover label and so 4px high everywhere except the one breakpoint where the padding arithmetic happened to agree — and then that ring **re-cut as an actual orbit** on the owner's report, which found it passing over the laptop rather than around it, outgrowing the screen between 480 and 639px, and changing its tilt three times on the way from a phone to a desktop: depth is now decided per button per frame (behind the laptop on the far half of each lap, in front on the near half) instead of once for the whole ring, and the six responsive bands collapse into one ellipse whose width the viewport dictates and whose tilt is the same 0.204 at every size — then, on the owner's third report, the whole hero re-proportioned as one unit: the orbit anchored to the laptop's measured base and raised 15% of the laptop's own height above it, the laptop's flat tablet rung replaced by a 432→480px ramp (and a 480→512 xl ramp from 1536, rings and orbit cap scaling with it), and the laptop's height bound made to reserve the ground rings' room (`max-height: min(100%, 75% + 35px)`) so a height-crunched desktop shrinks laptop and rings together in the lg proportion instead of letting a full-size laptop swallow rings shrunk to 0.6 — plus the dead-band width ramp resurrected from a cascade fault, the ring-fit read fixed for `calc()` rungs via `@property`, and the contact shadow re-cut as a fixed 0.875 ratio of the laptop's measured width; and the hero's **640–767px dead band** interpolated on the same owner's report that it "looks unprofessional" there — Tailwind's `sm` is a 128px-wide plateau on this page, so the nav icon sat at its phone size (24px) in a button that had grown to 64, the title and role line held one value across the whole band, and the laptop — the only size expressed as a percentage rather than a step — kept growing to 76% of the viewport before snapping down 226px at `md`, which is what made everything pinned underneath it read as shrunken; twelve sizes now ramp continuously from the value the band already had at 640 to the exact `md` value they hand over to at 768, so the cliff stops existing rather than shrinking, with the icon re-based so its share of the button no longer goes *backwards* at 640 and the contact shadow re-derived from the 0.85 ratio the larger breakpoints keep; and — found while measuring that band — the same hero given a **vertical fit**, the hero having been sized only ever by width on a shell that cannot scroll, so a 667x375 landscape phone lost the orbit and half the laptop below a fold they could not be scrolled to (+189px past it, and +19px even at 1440x700, which makes it a 1366x768 laptop bug as much as a phone one), fixed by capping the stage with `max-*` where CSS allows it and with `min()` ladders where it does not, by letting the hero row shrink at all (`flex-1` carries `min-height: auto` and was growing off-screen instead), and by giving the orbit a third limit on `a` measured off the ring's own live centre — the tilt being owner-fixed, a ring that must be shorter has to be narrower; and then the same hero's laptop stopped from ever **sitting on the role line's banknote plate and the word ENGINEER**, reported from a 13" MacBook Pro and found on sweep at 12 of 18 viewports (the printed text itself at four), which had survived two rounds of measurement because both checked only the bottom fold while the hero row — `flex-1`, so `min-height: auto`, so unable to shrink — was spilling its centred cluster equally in BOTH directions, and because the plate is an absolutely positioned canvas whose lower overhang was reserved only by an assumption that the laptop's half of the hero would always be empty: the overhang is now reserved symmetrically with the upper one, and the laptop is bounded structurally rather than by ramp (`width: auto` + `max-width` + `max-height`, the one configuration where CSS satisfies both limits and keeps the aspect ratio), so it cannot be taller than the space between the headline and the fold at any viewport; and finally the same hero given permission to be **taller than the screen** on the heights where it genuinely cannot fit, the orbit having still hung 28px off the bottom at 1440x340 on the owner's report that "the rings go out on some screen heights" — the ring sizes itself to fit but is floored at `MIN_SEMI_AXIS`, and past that floor the shortfall was simply amputated by `h-screen overflow-hidden`, so the shell becomes `min-h-screen`, the hero row reserves the room the navigation is guaranteed (the component's own arithmetic read backwards), and the page scrolls to the remainder instead of losing it; and, once it could scroll, the hero's caps given a **floor** (`--hero-min`, 580px, swept against ring crowding and the scroll it costs) so a short viewport stops being crushed into one it no longer has to fit — the orbit at 844x390 going from its size floor with eight buttons piled on a 264px ellipse to the same width limit a desktop has, with the laptop 129px -> 240px — and the hover label that the ring was never sized around flipped above the button on the handful of positions where it would otherwise fall off the foot of the page; and finally the GROUND rings brought inside the hero too, reported from a 13" MacBook Pro where the outermost ripple ran 80px past the bottom edge — decorative, so outside the orbit's fit arithmetic, and above the height at which the cap block would have shrunk them — the three rings now scaled by one factor until the outermost closes on its gutter, applied to their BOX rather than their painted result because the perspective divisor relaxes as the ring shrinks (worth 22 points of ring), and applied instead of the page-lengthening first attempt, which left the first view cut exactly as before while adding a scrollbar to every laptop; and the orbit tooltip's dead `md:text-md` class removed — never a Tailwind class in this config, so it emitted nothing and only read as a breakpoint step that does not exist; and two hero documentation claims brought back in step with the code — the `.hero-row` prose in `page.js` and the README still describing the retired `min-height: 0` floor, and the README's flipped-label classes missing the sign on `-mb-1`; and the `--ring-size` registration's `0px` initial disarmed in the ladder's fractional media gaps by a base rung written one specificity step below the rungs; and the orbit's hover label given to keyboard users — revealed on `group-focus-visible`, with focus holding the same rotation pause hover does, so the tabbed-to label is as flip-stable as the hovered one; and the homepage's webfont re-measure given the unmount flag the orbit's twin already had, with the orbit-sizing comment corrected to say WHICH height left the arithmetic — the viewport's, not the row's own box, which deliberately still caps `a`; and the site given a **living shared-link identity** touch-drag on the ring band, the laptop itself as a vertical scrubber (hover-driven wherever a hover-capable fine pointer exists, including an iPad the moment a trackpad attaches; touch-drag-driven where one does not), and ← / → from any focused button, all feeding the one rotation the ambient spin writes, which now pauses while the visitor is engaged and eases back in after a quiet period — with a once-per-visitor, `localStorage`-remembered accessibility tip that announces politely, dismisses by ×, Esc, or the first real interaction, and re-words itself for the sub-480px column layout — site cursor grows breathing ↑ ↓ chevrons over the laptop, and the tip re-written to lead with the laptop gesture in plain words beside an animated pictogram, worded per input capability; and the brand-seal share-card rework ([#88](https://github.com/MA1002643/theabdullahfolio/issues/88) follow-up) — every OG card, both app icons, and a new `/my-past` archive route re-cut around the full over the complete CV + LinkedIn record (every role, qualification and volunteering post, 2018 → now), and the orbital ring's GitHub slot handed to the new route; and the orbital-nav tip silenced below 480px ([#105](https://github.com/MA1002643/theabdullahfolio/issues/105) follow-up) — on a phone the ring does not exist, so no telling of it belongs there, and a column tap no longer burns the persisted showing a desktop visit is owed — then made airtight on review: the discovery signal is ring-only at the source (the never-cleared column-tap latch used to burn the showing anyway on the next rotation into ≥480px) and Esc dismisses only a tip that is actually rendered; and the dev server made viewable from a real phone — the CSP's `upgrade-insecure-requests` now ships in production only, because real WebKit honours it even over `http://`, failing every asset upgrade and rendering each dev page as a page-tall grey void; and the `/guestbook` interactive neon guestbook ([#40](https://github.com/MA1002643/theabdullahfolio/issues/40)) — GitHub-identity messages over a json/Redis storage-driver pair, hand-drawn ink signatures stored as strictly-validated SVG paths, reactions, live presence, and the shared ⌘K command-palette primitive, with Guestbook replacing LinkedIn in the nav ring and the repo's first unit/e2e test infrastructure (vitest + Playwright), its composer later inheriting the contact message field's intelligence layer (guestbook-mode AI refine, draft autosave/restore, offline hold), and the recruiter-legibility pass (skeleton wall, ghost-composer parity, deep-linkable marks, mocked-session e2e coverage), and a review pass (the e2e script now really builds first, a forced Redis driver without credentials fails fast instead of null-dereferencing on the first request, the share-card copy names both sign-in providers, and the bundle analyzer is loaded only when `ANALYZE=true` so a devDependency-free `next start` no longer crashes, Playwright only reuses a server on port 3100 when explicitly told to, and anonymous presence heartbeats are capped per IP so a unique-id flood can no longer inflate the live count or hammer Redis, a deep link whose hash will not percent-decode is ignored instead of crashing the wall, and the presence id is minted without depending on the secure-context-only `crypto.randomUUID`, so the wall survives plain-http LAN viewing, and the signature pad's point cap is derived from the server's 4 KB grammar cap so it can no longer accept a stroke the route rejects, the pad rescales its ink when its box resizes so the signature drawn is the signature shipped, and the json driver's rate limit reserves the slot in the same synchronous step as the check so concurrent posts by one user can no longer both pass, and the Playwright server's env empties every Redis credential name so the smoke run's presence heartbeats stay in memory instead of reaching the live store, and the json driver serialises every mutation through one queue and writes by atomic rename so concurrent posts, deletes and reactions can no longer overwrite one another), plus one finding on `/journey` (a keyboard-driven atlas or palette jump now outlines the card it lands focus on, where the indicator had been suppressed outright), and one on the shared ⌘K palette (the hotkey pressed while open now closes through the same path as Esc — query cleared, selection reset, focus handed back), and CI now runs both test suites (`npm test`, then Playwright against the job's own build) so every one of these regressions gates a pull request; and the `/journey` polish pass ([#38](https://github.com/MA1002643/theabdullahfolio/issues/38) follow-up, a 16-point owner correction list) — the page recoloured onto the site's four established voices (page-title ember, eyebrow amber, Architect-paragraph amber, subtitle pink), the atlas's open bars clamped exactly to the NOW line, the comet's fire tail removed, the era pills straddling the spine at every width, the per-type card edge strip retired for `/about`'s uniform gold frame, and the footer's Index board re-cut into two columns — then the page's information layer deepened on owner approval: sticky atlas lane labels, per-card tenure readouts, a live "running today" concurrency line, an end-cap tally with the CV hand-off, and `/journey`'s own ⌘K palette action set; and a second correction round — the page's body copy re-inked in the contact intro's per-word fire ramp (a new shared `FireText`), card titles onto the page-title ember, the atlas scale widened so every open bar holds its caption inside, the card glow un-clipped on the section edge, and the sticky lane labels re-cut as full-height panels. Each change below is its own table — field labels on the left, full detail on the right._

### Added

#### `/guestbook` — an interactive neon guestbook: GitHub-identity messages, drawn ink signatures, and a flag-gated elite layer

| | |
|:--|:--|
| **Ref** | [#40](https://github.com/MA1002643/theabdullahfolio/issues/40), built with three owner-approved corrections to the issue as written: Auth.js **v5** patterns replace the issue's v4 snippets (`src/auth.js` exporting `{ handlers, auth, signIn, signOut }`, the route re-exporting `handlers`, `await auth()` server-side — never `getServerSession`); the issue's `writeFileSync`-to-JSON storage is a **driver interface from day one** because Vercel's filesystem is ephemeral; and the issue's <5KB JS budget is replaced by an explicit ≤65KB gzipped budget with the shader chunk lazy and excluded. Owner-directed besides: no commits until manual review, the command palette built as a **shared** component for a separate sitewide-palette issue, no View Transitions work (the existing Stone Passage already covers the route), and the headline/background following the sitewide patterns rather than the issue's stale markup |
| **Files** | **new** `src/auth.js`, `src/app/api/auth/[...nextauth]/route.js`, `src/app/api/guestbook/route.js` (+ `reactions/`, `presence/`), `src/app/(sub pages)/guestbook/page.js` (+ `opengraph-image.js`, `twitter-image.js`, `layout.js` metadata pass-through), `src/lib/flags.js`, `src/lib/guestbook/` (`store.js`, `jsonDriver.js`, `redisDriver.js`, `validate.js`, `ratelimit.js`, `timeAgo.js`, `signature.js`, `reactions.js`, `presence.js`, `prefs.js`, `events.js`), `src/components/guestbook/` (13 components), `src/components/commandPalette/CommandPalette.jsx`, `src/hooks/` (`useGuestbookMessages`, `useSignaturePad`, `useScramble`, `usePresence`, `useUiSound`, `useGuestbookPrefs`, `useCommandPalette`), `data/guestbook.json` (local dev data — gitignored, never committed), `vitest.config.mjs`, `playwright.config.mjs`, `tests/unit/` (5 suites, 32 tests), `tests/e2e/guestbook.spec.js` · `src/app/data.js`, `src/components/navigation/NavButton.jsx`, `src/components/footer/footer-data.js`, `src/app/globals.css`, `next.config.mjs` (bundle-analyzer wrapper only), `package.json`, `.env.example`, `.gitignore`, `README.md` |
| **Details** | **Identity is the spam defence.** Reading the wall is public; writing requires a GitHub session (next-auth pinned at `5.0.0-beta.32`), and the author's name/username/avatar come from the session **only** — anything the request body claims about identity is discarded. On top of that: 2–150-char validation on the trimmed value with control characters rejected outright (the input is single-line, so their presence proves the UI was bypassed), a URL/link-shortener filter and a small normalised profanity list (leet-mapped, repeat-collapsed — a unit test caught that collapsing runs to *two* letters let "fuuuuck" through; runs now collapse to one, and Scunthorpe-style innocents are asserted safe), and a server-side rate limit of one message per user per five minutes — `@upstash/ratelimit` sliding-window on the Redis driver, a newest-post timestamp check on the dev driver, both returning an honest `Retry-After`. `GUESTBOOK_ADMIN` names the one username whose session may `DELETE` a message. **Storage is a two-driver contract** (`getMessages / addMessage / deleteMessage / getReactions / setReaction`): Upstash Redis in production (ZSET index + one key per message + one reactions HASH per message, on the KV store the site already runs) selected automatically when `KV_REST_API_URL` is present, and a dev-only JSON file behind `GUESTBOOK_DRIVER=json`; the API routes never import `fs`. Who-reacted stays server-private — only aggregate counts and the caller's own choice ever leave. **The ink signature is the page's signature feature.** A pointer-events canvas (mouse/finger/pen — `touch-action:none` is what makes finger-drawing possible) records strokes with velocity-modulated width and quadratic midpoint smoothing, then serialises the centreline into a 100×40-normalised path using ONLY `M/L/Q/C/Z`, numbers 0–100 at ≤2 decimals, spaces and commas — a grammar enforced by a command-aware tokenizer (correct arity per command, ≤4KB measured in bytes, ≤400 commands, ≥1 drawing command) that runs **twice**: in the API before storage and again in `SignatureGlyph` before render, so nothing reaches the DOM that didn't parse moments earlier. Point capture is budgeted (3px min spacing, 160-point cap) so the client cannot draw something the server would reject. On cards the glyph draws itself on via framer's `pathLength` 0→1 with an ember drop-shadow, timed after the card's entrance; keyboard-only and reduced-motion visitors get four preset marks as real radio inputs — each asserted valid against the same grammar in the unit suite. **Everything flashy sits behind `src/lib/flags.js`** — one boolean each, checked at mount points, so any feature dies with a one-line flip: the ambient **ember-field shader** (an R3F sibling of AuroraDust — three was already a dependency, so no `ogl` — 5-octave domain-warped fBm over the Tailwind ember ramp with rising flicker-motes, the whole field *leaning* toward the cursor via an inertia-eased drift uniform, DPR capped at 1.5, frameloop paused on hidden tab/off-screen, never mounted under reduced motion, on <4 logical cores, or before a post-reveal idle callback — that idle gate alone took mobile TBT from 1,550ms to 450ms — then flipped **off** on owner review so the backdrop is /about's exactly, the shared AuroraDust aurora rendering via the flag's own kill-switch fallback; the field stays one flip away); card **tilt + specular glare** and **magnetic pull** on Send/CTA reusing the existing `useCardTilt`/`useMagneticPull` contracts; the **scramble-decode headline** (visually identical to PageTitle via the `.page-title-*` classes, SSR-rendering the real text so hydration and SEO never see glyph soup, re-decoding whenever a new message lands — signalled by a window CustomEvent so nothing is prop-drilled — then flipped **off** on owner review so the title and subtitle play the exact sitewide PageTitle ignite with `replayOnView`, same as every other sub-page; the variant stays one flip away); **scroll choreography** (cards low in the viewport carry a blur+brightness *filter* falloff that sharpens as they rise — filter, not opacity, because opacity belongs to the entrance/exit animations and a style motion-value would override them; the background scroll drift the flag originally covered was removed outright on the same owner review — the /about backdrop the page must mirror is static); **presence + polling** (a ZSET of anonymous per-tab heartbeat ids in a 60s window drives the "N here now" pill in the header meta strip; the same flag re-reads the wall every 30s, merge-not-stomp so pending optimistic cards survive and arrivals get the glow + aria-live announcement + headline re-scramble; every timer skips hidden tabs); **reactions** (🔥 🚀 ❤️, optimistic with exact-object restore on failure, spring count roll-ups, and a 40-line hand-rolled canvas ember burst instead of canvas-confetti); the **⌘K command palette** — a genuinely shared component + hook pair (`src/components/commandPalette/`, `useCommandPalette`) that knows nothing about the guestbook: actions arrive as config (`{ id, label, hint, section, keywords, perform }`), navigation goes through the Stone Passage's `usePageTransition().navigate`, and the a11y shape is the combobox pattern (input as the single trapped tab stop, `aria-activedescendant` over a listbox, Esc closes, focus restored); **UI sounds** (four synthesised Web Audio blips — focus/send/reaction/error — OFF by default, persisted in a `useSyncExternalStore`-backed prefs store, the AudioContext not even constructed until an enabled, gesture-driven play); a manual **motion toggle** in the palette that wraps the page in `MotionConfig reducedMotion="always"` and unmounts the shader; and a **grain + vignette** finish from an inline feTurbulence data-URI. **The optimistic flow is the UX spine:** submit inserts a pending card instantly (shimmer via a compositor-only `::after` sweep), swaps in the server object or rolls back restoring the typed draft, sonner toasts both ways, and the wall is a real `<ul>/<li>` with an `aria-live="polite"` region. Relative timestamps are a ~15-line `timeAgo` (no date library) re-rendered once a minute, with clock-skew clamped and a `29d`→absolute-date boundary. **Navigation:** Guestbook replaces LinkedIn **in place** at ring slot 6 — the sub-480px layout slices `BtnList` 0-3/4-7, so appending would have silently dropped a button on phones — with `MessageSquare` in the `getIcon` switch; LinkedIn keeps its footer ELSEWHERE slot, and the footer's quickLinks + Manifest departures board gain the route (`GBK` board code). **Quality gates, measured:** `/guestbook` first load is 177KB against /about's 166 and /projects' 176 (page chunk 18.5KB; the three/R3F chunk is lazy and outside first load; shared 87.4KB byte-identical before/after). Lighthouse mobile (simulated, production server): CLS **0.399 → 0.028** after reserving `min-h` under a short wall (the shift was the *footer's* late-assembling masthead entering the first viewport on the empty state — /about hides the same shift only by being tall) and perf 25 → **62 — exact parity with /about (62)**; accessibility 96 on both, capped by three pre-existing footer `aria-prohibited-attr` nodes — the guestbook tree contributes zero failures, after an in-tree contrast pass raised every informational `text-foreground/40–50` token to `/60` (measured ≥5.6:1 on the effective ground; /50 computes to 4.3:1 and fails). All animation is transform/opacity/filter; every piece gates on the shared `useReducedMotion`. **Tests are the repo's first:** vitest (32 tests over `timeAgo`, the signature grammar — including markup/CSS smuggles, arity violations and the byte cap — the validator, the rate limiter, and the storage-driver contract run against the JSON driver in a temp dir via `GUESTBOOK_JSON_PATH`) and a Playwright smoke suite booting a production server on port 3100 (unauthenticated wall renders read-only, sign-in CTA visible, `POST` without a session → 401). New deps justified: `next-auth@5.0.0-beta.32` (pinned exact — beta channel; ~6KB client), `@upstash/ratelimit` (server-only); dev: `vitest`, `@playwright/test`, `@next/bundle-analyzer` (wired behind `ANALYZE=true`, `openAnalyzer:false`). Known limits, stated: the analyzer path trips over the pre-existing `/api/experience-summary` pdf-parse externals during its collect phase (normal builds unaffected); Lighthouse ≥90/100 is unreachable on ANY sub-page of this site under mobile simulation while the intro loader gates LCP and the footer carries its aria attrs — both outside this issue's file scope. **Follow-up (owner-directed): Google sign-in joins GitHub** — same `src/auth.js` module (`AUTH_GOOGLE_ID`/`AUTH_GOOGLE_SECRET`, one Google OAuth client carrying both redirect URIs, unlike GitHub's one-callback apps). Google identities are keyed internally as `google:<sub>` — the colon is illegal in GitHub logins, so the two namespaces (and the `GUESTBOOK_ADMIN` check) can never collide — and the author model gains a `provider` field the cards present by: GitHub authors keep the linked avatar + `@handle`, Google authors show their display name with an unlinked avatar (no public profile to point at, and the internal id never renders — the optimistic pending card mirrors the same rule). The CTA becomes a two-button pair ("Sign in to leave a message") with `react-icons/fa` brand marks replacing lucide's deprecated `Github` glyph; reaction/palette hint copy drops the GitHub-specific wording; the smoke test asserts both buttons. **Follow-up (owner-directed): the header meta strip** — the plain "N messages and counting" line + presence pill above the composer replaced by the unified three-pill row from the owner's concept comp: live presence (ping dot, "N here now"), the mark count ("N marks left"), and a "⌘K · commands" hint — the comp's elements re-clothed in the site's own chrome — the accent triad one pill each (pink presence, gold count, ember ⌘K; the neutral-grey first cut was rejected as off-theme on a colourful site), semibold tabular figures, mono microcopy on the shared glassy pill shell, with the ⌘K pill double-gated — behind the palette flag, and behind a new `useHardwareKeyboard` hook (owner-directed refinement over the first cut's sm-breakpoint hiding, which was viewport-width, not capability): the pill shows only where a physical keyboard is plausible, combining the site's reactive `(hover: hover) and (pointer: fine)` query (desktop, or an iPad live-flipping as a trackpad attaches) with a latched hardware-key proof (any keydown while focus is outside an editable element — on-screen keyboards can only type into one, so Tab/arrows/⌘K in open page space prove real keys, catching the keyboard-only iPad the pointer query misses), SSR-safe by defaulting hidden — and the strip keeping its no-flash loading gate plus a reduced-motion-aware rise that now REPLAYS on every return into view (the headline's replayOnView manners, via `whileInView` rather than a ref-observed `useInView` because the strip mounts after the first fetch, when a mount-effect-observed ref would still be null); the empty state re-clothed in the same triad on the following owner beat — the flat-grey MessageSquare now an ember icon in an ember-ringed, softly haloed disc, "No messages yet" in the chrome gold, and "Be the first to leave your mark" in the subtitle's pink, a deliberate typographic-and-chromatic echo of the headline's LEAVE YOUR MARK; the sign-in card's grey "Sign in to leave a message" lead recoloured to the same invitational pink on the next beat, completing the pink-invitation → gold-action hierarchy above its gold CTA pair. **Follow-up (owner-directed): the wall paginated as an instrument** — a 150-mark wall must not mount 150 animated cards, and at guestbook scale the payload was never the bottleneck (~45KB of JSON, growth rate-limited to one message per user per five minutes) while the DOM was (every card is a framer `li` with entrance choreography, a scroll-linked depth filter, tilt handlers and sometimes a self-drawing signature SVG) — so the fetch-all data layer (poll-merge, optimistic submit, reactions) is untouched and the RENDER is paged: eight cards per leaf, flipped by a pagination built from the site's own instruments rather than numbered buttons — a graduation-ticked rail (the journey dial's language) carrying an ember filament and glowing comet head (the journey spine's comet recast horizontal), the WHOLE rail one generous click-to-jump hit target instead of nineteen 4px ticks; a two-digit odometer that rolls with the travel direction (the ghost-year manners); prev/next in the sign-in CTA's pill chrome. The flip itself is `mode="wait"`: the old leaf slips out along the travel direction and the incoming page replays the wall's own staggered card entrance — the flip IS the existing choreography. Posting flips to leaf one so the visitor watches their own pending shimmer from wherever they composed; the page clamps back into range if deletes empty the last leaf; arrival glows retire on flip (a new `clearNewIds` seam — and the `initialIds` set is gone outright, since a paginated mount cohort staggers by in-page index and a solo arrival at index 0 has zero delay by construction). A11y: the rail is decorative-plus-pointer (`aria-hidden`) with the two real buttons as the canonical path and a visually-hidden aria-live "Page N of M" announced once per flip; reduced motion drops the springs, the digit roll and the leaf slide. Found while seeding dev data to exercise the pagination: the README already described `data/guestbook.json` as gitignored but no rule existed — the dev wall (visitor test posts, seeded messages) was one `git add .` from the public repo; `/data/guestbook.json` added to `.gitignore` to make the claim true. **Follow-up (owner-directed): the wall's card pass** — six refinements in one beat: (1) every card gains a LIVE scroll reveal (the journey nodes' manners — `useInView` re-arms off-screen so the entrance replays on every return, with the cohort stagger applying only to a card's FIRST reveal; a lone card scrolling back answers immediately), which also stops below-fold cards from burning their entrance unseen; (2) message bodies move off grey onto the share cards' warm parchment ink (`#f2e2cd`/90); (3) author names take the headline's own `#ff6d05` — content speaks in the title's ink — with timestamps and the avatar hover-handle on the pink meta voice; (4) **authors can delete their own message**: a quiet bin button on their card (pink at rest, ember on intent), an optimistic removal with exact-position rollback and success/failure toasts, and the DELETE route broadened from admin-only to own-or-admin with ownership re-derived server-side from the session (README updated); (5) the composer's avatar gains the cards' initial-letter fallback disc so an imageless session still shows who is writing (real GitHub/Google avatars already flowed through both card and composer); (6) the sign-in card rebuilt as GLASS OVER THE GOAL — an inert, aria-hidden ghost of the actual composer (input row, counter, send, signature pad) sits dimmed beneath a frosted `backdrop-blur` pane carrying the pink invitation and the gold OAuth pair, so the locked state shows exactly what signing in unlocks instead of a black wall |

#### `/guestbook` — composer re-laid to the concept comp, message ink unified with the contact form, and the owner recognised as the wall's moderator

| | |
|:--|:--|
| **Ref** | [#40](https://github.com/MA1002643/theabdullahfolio/issues/40) follow-up, owner-directed: the compose card re-laid after the guestbook concept comp (keeping the existing show/hide signature toggle), every text colour pulled onto the site's own palette, a card-header layout fix, and admin moderation surfaced in the UI. |
| **Files** | **new** `src/lib/guestbook/admin.js`, `src/hooks/useCountUp.js`, `tests/unit/admin.test.js`; **changed** `src/auth.js`, `src/app/api/guestbook/route.js`, `src/components/guestbook/MessageInput.jsx`, `src/components/guestbook/SignatureField.jsx`, `src/components/guestbook/MessageCard.jsx`, `src/components/guestbook/GuestbookWall.jsx`, `src/components/guestbook/PresencePill.jsx`, `src/hooks/useGuestbookMessages.js`, `src/hooks/useSignaturePad.js`, `src/app/globals.css`. |
| **Details** | **Composer.** The compose card gains the wall cards' specular glare — deliberately NOT their tilt: the signature pad maps pointer coordinates through `getBoundingClientRect()`, which reflects CSS transforms, so a card rotating in 3D under the cursor makes ink land away from the pointer (`useCardTilt` exposes rotation and glare separately, so the glare tracks while the card stays flat). It now types in the contact form's one input voice: the message field is the contact page's `FireInput` — fire-amber gradient glyphs, flat `#ff6d05` placeholder and caret — with programmatic clear/restore going through `setNativeValue` so the gradient overlay never drifts from the real value; the `0/150` counter and the card timestamps read in the feature cards' numeral ember `#ff6d05` (the pagination digits' / "Projects shipped" ink — one colour for every number; the counter's near-cap warning survives as a neon glow intensification rather than a colour change); the send button becomes the comp's bordered square, its icon in the action ember `#ff6d05` (the contact CTA's colour) with the same ember-glow hover (scoped to `:enabled`); the signature slot folds under a dashed gold divider with the toggle as its header label (pink at rest, ember on hover — the nav buttons' flip), Clear promoted to a header action, the pad's border turning from dashed gold to solid ember the moment a stroke lands, and the comp's centred hint line ("stroke weight follows your speed" — true of `useSignaturePad`). The preset-mark radios are REMOVED outright (owner call): drawing is the one signature path, and on `(any-pointer: none)` devices the whole section renders nothing. **Ink-accuracy fix.** `useSignaturePad` sized its bitmap in a mount effect reading `canvasRef.current` — but the canvas mounts LATE (only when the panel opens), so the effect found null, the ResizeObserver never attached, and the canvas kept the HTML-default 300×150 bitmap that CSS then stretched ~2× — every stroke displayed magnified away from the cursor. Sizing now rides a CALLBACK ref, so the fit and observer bind the moment the canvas actually attaches; verified pixel-level (ink bbox within ~2px of the pointer path). **Count-up pills.** "N here now" and "N marks left" roll in with the site's elite count-up (new `useCountUp`, wrapping the stat cards' `animateToTarget` sprint-then-settle): full 0→N on entrance, a quick 600ms roll on live changes, instant under reduced motion — with the plural following the real count so the label never reads "1 marks". The climb REPLAYS on every scroll back into view (owner-directed), re-armed by the strip's in-view state — tracked with a callback ref + IntersectionObserver at the whileInView rise's own 0.35 threshold, NOT framer's useInView-on-a-ref, which would find a null ref on the late-mounting strip (the signature pad's late-mount trap again). The marks pill re-inks in the Career Snapshot's "Personal Projects" eyebrow amber `#ffaa2a`, text and border both (owner call). **Signatures draw on arrival.** SignatureGlyph gains a `play` gate wired to its card's existing in-view state: a below-fold signature holds un-drawn at page load, inks itself only when its card scrolls into view, and re-arms with the card's replayed entrance — timed off `entranceDelay` (not the mount stagger) so a re-entry draws immediately, not index-in-line. **Numerals & fire-ramp labels (owner-directed).** The pagination odometer's digits ("01 / 03") re-ink in the feature cards' numeral colour — the vivid `#ff6d05` of the "Projects shipped" / years digits — so numbers speak in one colour across the site's cards ("PAGE" and "/" stay pink meta). The signature toggle ("Add a signature (optional)") and the pad's hint line take the contact intro's exact ink: the shared gold→ember fire ramp (`wordFill` from `@/lib/fireRamp`), applied per word so each line darkens in reading order like "Step into the circle of enchantment…" — per-word clips being the GPU-safe form of the fill. The toggle still flips solid action-ember on hover/focus via `.gb-fire-hover` (globals.css; `!important` out-ranks the words' inline clips), with the PenLine icon riding the ramp's bright-gold end and joining the flip by group-hover. **Wall.** Message bodies drop the parchment grey for `.gb-fire-ink` — the contact textarea's 180deg amber→ember gradient clipped to the glyphs, no glow (body copy, not a badge; clip-inside-animated-card follows the RepoStatsCard precedent). The card header loses `justify-between`, which had floated the timestamp toward the card's centre whenever the bin rendered: the author name takes `flex-1`, so the time stays pinned to the right edge and the bin only nudges it by its own width. **Moderation.** New `isAdminUsername()` (`GUESTBOOK_ADMIN`, case-insensitive because GitHub logins are; `google:<sub>` ids can never collide) now backs the DELETE route's own-or-admin gate AND is stamped onto the session (`session.user.isAdmin`, derived per-read so rotating the env var takes effect immediately), so the owner sees the bin on **every** confirmed card — with the aria-label naming whose message it deletes and the toast saying "Message removed" rather than "Your message" on a moderation delete. Six unit tests pin the helper (match, case-fold, unset/empty env, missing username, Google-namespace non-collision). |

#### `/guestbook` — the composer inherits the contact message field's intelligence layer: AI refine, draft autosave/restore, and an offline hold

| | |
|:--|:--|
| **Ref** | [#40](https://github.com/MA1002643/theabdullahfolio/issues/40) follow-up, owner-directed: the compose card should carry the same features the contact form's message field has — the streaming AI rewrite, the autosaved draft with its restore banner, and the connection-awareness — rather than being the one message field on the site without them. |
| **Files** | **new** `src/hooks/useFieldDraft.js`; **changed** `src/app/api/refine-message/route.js`, `src/hooks/useMessageRefine.js`, `src/components/contact/MessageRefine.jsx`, `src/components/guestbook/MessageInput.jsx` |
| **Details** | **One refine endpoint, two editorial contracts.** `/api/refine-message` gains an optional `mode` in the body: `guestbook` selects its own system prompt (a casual one-line public mark, ≤`MESSAGE_MAX` chars, single line, never add links), its own length gates (8–150 vs the contact 20–2000) and a smaller output-token cap (120 vs 400), plus its own gateway cost tag (`feature:guestbook-refine`); an absent or unknown mode falls back to the contact contract, so the deployed contact form's requests are untouched. Everything else — the per-IP rate limit, the drain-until-first-token error surfacing, the abort plumbing — stays one implementation. `useMessageRefine({ mode })` threads the field through (sent only when set), and `MessageRefine` itself grows `mode` + `minLength` props so the guestbook reuses the exact panel the way it already reuses `FireInput` — same affordance, same streaming ghost, same Use this / Polish again / Discard set. An accepted rewrite is whitespace-collapsed and hard-capped at `MESSAGE_MAX` before it lands in the field. **Draft that mirrors the field.** `useFieldDraft` is `useFormDraft` re-cut for a single controlled input: debounced (600ms) autosave to `guestbook:draft:v1` with the same 7-day TTL, restore-on-mount through `setNativeValue` so the FireInput gradient overlay repaints in the same dispatch, and the contact form's "Restored your unsent message" banner (same `draft-restored` classes, same Keep/Clear pair) at the top of the card. Because the autosave keys on the field's value, storage is self-cleaning: the optimistic clear on submit deletes the draft and a failed post's restore re-saves it, with no clearStored() choreography. **Offline hold, not an offline queue — deliberately.** Sending while offline keeps the text in the field (and therefore the draft) behind an explanatory toast instead of spending the optimistic card against a dead connection. The contact form's auto-send queue was consciously NOT ported: a guestbook post is an authenticated, 1-per-5-min rate-limited action, so a background resend could fire against an expired session or a fresh rate limit — holding the visitor's words where they can see them is the honest hold. |

#### `/guestbook` — the recruiter-legibility pass: skeleton wall, ghost-composer parity, deep-linkable marks, and test depth

| | |
|:--|:--|
| **Ref** | Owner-directed follow-up to the composer-intelligence work: an audit of the whole page concluded the features were enough — the remaining wins were making the auth-gated sophistication visible to a visitor who never signs in, closing the one blank loading moment, and pinning the new logic under tests. |
| **Files** | **new** `src/lib/refineModes.js`, `tests/unit/refineModes.test.js`, `tests/unit/useFieldDraft.test.js`, `tests/e2e/guestbook-composer.spec.js`; **changed** `src/app/api/refine-message/route.js`, `src/components/guestbook/GuestbookWall.jsx`, `src/components/guestbook/MessageCard.jsx`, `src/components/guestbook/SignInPrompt.jsx`, `package.json` (dev-only: `jsdom`, `@testing-library/react`) |
| **Details** | **Skeleton wall.** The first fetch used to render nothing — a blank gap between headline and footer on every slow connection. Three ghost cards in the wall's own card language now hold the space, wearing the optimistic post's `.gb-pending` shimmer; the block fades in after a 200ms delay so a fast load never flashes it, the middle ghost runs a line longer so the trio reads as content rather than pattern, and AT hears one sr-only "Loading the guestbook…" instead of three empty cards. **Ghost-composer parity.** The frosted sign-in pane's inert preview previously advertised an outdated form; it now mirrors the real composer's full feature set — the ✦ "Refine with AI" affordance where the live one grows, the signature fold with its actual header line, a "Drafts save themselves as you type" hint, and the character counter derived from `MESSAGE_MAX` instead of a hard-coded `0/150` — so a recruiter who never signs in still learns what is behind the glass. **Deep-linkable marks.** Every confirmed card carries its message id as a DOM id plus a quiet copy-link button (the bin's colour voice; always visible — hover-reveal would strand touch screens) that copies `/guestbook#msg_…`. A visit to such a URL finds the message in the first loaded list, flips the paginated wall to its page, scrolls the card to reading height after the leaf-flip choreography settles, and replays the ignite glow on it; a hash matching nothing (deleted mark, mangled link) is a silent no-op, and a page flip retires the marker like any other arrival glow. **Test depth.** The refine-mode table moved out of the route into `src/lib/refineModes.js` (pure data + lookup, the validate.js doctrine) — which also fixed a latent lookup bug: `MODES[mode] ?? fallback` returned truthy prototype junk for `"__proto__"`/`"constructor"` off the wire, now refused via `Object.hasOwn`. Unit tests pin the mode contracts (fallbacks, hostile keys, guestbook↔`MESSAGE_MAX` lock-step, contact back-compat, distinct gateway tags) and the draft hook's full lifecycle (restore, TTL expiry, debounce, submit-clear, Keep/Clear) under a per-file jsdom override. A new Playwright spec covers what the signed-out smoke test cannot: `/api/auth/session` is stubbed at the network layer so the real composer renders, asserting the counter, the refine affordance, draft autosave + the restore banner across a reload, the skeleton under a slowed fetch, and the deep link flipping to page 2 — all hermetic, no OAuth, no store state. |

#### `/journey` — the career told as a scroll-charged timeline, and the ring's GitHub slot handed to it

| | |
|:--|:--|
| **Ref** | [#38](https://github.com/MA1002643/theabdullahfolio/issues/38), plus owner direction in two beats: replace the orbital ring's GitHub button with the timeline's and make the page read as engineered rather than templated — "so complex, elite, professional and jaw dropping" was the brief — then, on review, "you haven't included all the employments, educations … including any volunteering work", with the LinkedIn profile supplied as the authority; and finally "all the project listed should be removed … this Journey line is purely to present my education/employment journey. the projects page is listing all the project anyways", with the poetic 2018 "The first line" card cut on the same review; then a further owner beat on the same elite brief: replace the straight line with a snake line, and bend the right-edge year list into an arch "giving an impression of the clock" — "something that makes the recruiters freeze" |
| **Files** | **new** `src/app/(sub pages)/journey/page.js` + `opengraph-image.js` + `twitter-image.js` · **new** `src/components/journey/index.jsx` + `TimelineNode.jsx` + `TimelineSpine.jsx` + `MilestoneMarker.jsx` + `GhostYear.jsx` + `EraRail.jsx` + `accents.js` + `spineGeometry.js` · `src/app/data.js` (`journeyData` + the `BtnList` swap) · `src/components/navigation/NavButton.jsx` + `index.jsx` · `src/components/pageTransition/constants.js` · `src/components/footer/footer-data.js` · `src/components/not-found/routes.js` + `NotFoundClient.jsx` · `src/app/(sub pages)/layout.js` · `src/app/globals.css` · `README.md` |
| **Details** | **One scroll value, four instruments.** The page hangs off a single spring-smoothed scroll progress (the 62%-viewport line crossing the list): a serpentine spine — an SVG snake built in `spineGeometry.js` from chained half-sine bows sampled every ~6px of descent, tangent-continuous because the bow side alternates per segment and threaded EXACTLY through every marker centre (the parity works out so each bow bends away from the card below it; the ResizeObserver pass surveys the real marker wrappers via `data-spine-marker`, so a spacing tweak can never desync the curve) — whose ember → amethyst → gold gradient is a `userSpaceOnUse` stroke revealed by a scroll-linked `clip-path` inset on a wrapper div: clip, not `scaleY`, so each colour stays pinned to its era instead of the whole ramp squashing into the lit sliver, and clip, not `stroke-dashoffset`, so the reveal stays composited instead of repainting the stroke every scroll frame — and because the horizontal clip edge IS the 62% line the eras elect on, where an arc-length reveal would drift from it. A comet rides the curve through the geometry's own x(y) lookup (no `getTotalLength` DOM round-trips — the sample table is the source of truth), ROTATED to the local tangent so its tail trails along the path through every bend, under a perpetually pulsing NOW beacon at the spine head (the page's only looping animation); and because the smoothing spring overshoots [0,1] on a hard flick at either end of the scroll, the spine clamps the progress value inside each derived transform — inline, not through a chained clamped MotionValue, which would add a frame of lag to every write (the #47 chained-`useTransform` lesson) — so the clip can never render a negative inset and the comet can never ride past the spine's ends; and the two spine gradients take per-instance ids off `useId` (the contact form's SendingLabel idiom, colon-stripped so they stay valid inside `url(#…)`), so a second mounted copy can never capture the first's gradient references; and the card lift plus its station energise given to keyboard users — focus inside a card (the VIEW link) earns the same lift/swell/brighten hover does, via `:focus-visible` rather than `:focus-within` (VIEW opens a NEW tab, so a mouse click leaves focus behind and `:focus-within` would pin the lift after the pointer left), with the base-lift focus rule kept out of the `:hover` selector lists so an engine without `:has()` drops only the enhancement, and the reduced-motion overrides covering hover and focus alike; and the era election made single-shot per observer delivery — one IntersectionObserver callback can carry two newly-intersecting nodes (a dial jump landing on a card boundary; the band is 10% of the viewport, so adjacent nodes can share it), and the winner used to be whichever sat later in DOM order, an implicit tiebreak — now the node occupying the most of the band is elected once, by `intersectionRect` height rather than `intersectionRatio`, which divides by the node's own height and would hand the election to a short card covering less of the band. **Era instruments.** An IntersectionObserver band (the middle 10% of the viewport) elects the active era and drives two fixed layers: a ghost-year odometer — a `clamp(8rem, 21vw, 17rem)` outline-stroked figure behind the cards whose digit columns roll 0–9 strips on springs, the cards' own backdrop-blur softening whatever passes behind them into depth — and a right-edge era DIAL (lg+): the years ride the rim of a 300px-radius clock face whose CENTRE is parked off-screen right, so only an arch of bezel intrudes into the page — a hairline double ring, minute-style graduations subdividing each year gap, and ONE spring-driven rotor that turns the whole bezel on era change so the active year swings onto a fixed ember needle at the midline (base angle −i·STEP against rotor ρ = activeIndex·STEP means the active label always lands exactly horizontal while its neighbours tilt like numerals on a watch bezel). Labels read INWARD from the rim, gauge-style, so no year can clip off the viewport's right edge as the dial turns; years dim with LIVE angular distance from the needle (a `useTransform` off the rotor, not the elected era, so they fade mid-swing) and drop out of the tab order once they swing past the visible arc; placement is a plain-CSS `rotate(β) translateX(−R)` because framer composes translate before rotate — the wrong order for orbits — so framer only ever owns OTHER elements' properties (the rotor's rotation, each label's opacity/scale: the never-share-a-transform rule again); the mostly-off-screen 600px dial is `pointer-events-none` with only reachable year buttons opting back in; and clicking still `scrollIntoView`s the era. Both instruments fade with the timeline so neither ever floats over the footer. The same framer-owns-the-whole-transform rule turned up a latent bug the dial work exposed: the era separator pill's `-translate-x-1/2` classes were being overwritten by its scale animation (it sat half its width off-axis), so its centring moved to a plain wrapper — hanging off the rail on mobile, centred on the axis from md up — with framer animating only the inner span. **The instrument pass — everything live, nothing added.** A follow-up owner beat ("anything else to make it more elite … I really need to impress the recruiter") was answered by coupling the existing instruments to the scroll instead of bolting on ornament: (1) the dial is now WOUND BY THE SCROLL — the measure pass also surveys each era's first-marker y (`eraAnchors`), and the rail turns them into a continuous scroll↦angle map (piecewise-lerp, one spring) so the bezel creeps between eras like a coupled mechanism and each year swings onto the needle just as the IO election ignites it, with reduced motion dropping the winding (scroll-scrubbed SPATIAL rotation, the parallax category) for the old stepped snap; (2) the dial went TIME-TRUE — angles derive from year values, not list indices (β = −(2026 − year)·11.5°), so the 2021→2019 gap spans two steps and the silent 2020 exists as a position on the scale, marked by its own longer, brighter graduation; (3) the snake DRAWS ITSELF IN when the intro loader lifts — a one-shot `stroke-dashoffset` sweep over the geometry's own summed arc length (measured reality, not normalised `pathLength`), one-shot so the per-frame stroke-repaint cost that rules dashes out for the scroll reveal never applies, pre-drawn under reduced motion; and (4) hovering a card ENERGISES ITS STATION — pure-CSS `:has()` (progressive enhancement) swells the marker and brightens the connector, threading both #47 needles: the marker override repeats its class translate (a bare `transform` would drop it) and the connector is touched only through `filter`, never its framer-owned transform. A later owner beat — below lg the clock vanished entirely, and phones are where recruiters open links — kept the instrument on small screens as a DECORATIVE ECHO rather than complicating the cards (the offered alternative, declined as clutter against the page's restraint): the same `DialFace` renders twice off ONE shared rotor MotionValue (a MotionValue drives any number of subscribers, so both faces stay in lockstep for free) — the lg+ `<nav>` of buttons unchanged at z-30, and below lg an `aria-hidden`, `pointer-events-none` copy at −z-10 under the cards' backdrop-blur (the ghost-year depth trick) at half opacity, with `<span>`s where the buttons were — nothing focusable, nothing touchable, just the winding machinery showing through the gaps between cards. **Fifteen real milestones — the documented education/employment record, nothing else.** `journeyData` began as the CV's eleven engineering milestones, was rebuilt on owner review as the COMPLETE record from the supplied LinkedIn profile, and then re-scoped on the owner's later reviews to education/employment/volunteering ONLY — the six project cards (this site, AfaaqX, NutriTimer, Contacts-on-AWS, ArticleServer, culina) removed because `/projects` already owns the portfolio, and the poetic 2018 "The first line" origin card removed with them (the EST. 2018 · BOLTON, UK end-cap carries the where-it-began note instead), leaving the `project` and `origin` accents defined-but-untenanted in `accents.js` with the data-file comments pinning both decisions so nobody re-adds them. What stays is every employment (Independent Projects, Constant Security, C365Cloud, Adullam Homes, Showsec, Lidl, Unisys, the three MMU roles, KFC), every qualification (BSc, BTEC, and the OCNLR Digital Skills certificate the CV never listed), and the volunteering posts (Course Representative with its Learner Voice + Volunteering Awards, Student Ambassador), which give the amethyst `milestone` type real tenants; where the two sources disagreed LinkedIn won, moving the BTEC to 2019–2021 (the CV said 2017), the Unisys span to MAY 2023 — SEP 2024 (now with the team-lead / follow-on-contracts line), and the journey's start — end-cap and metadata included — from 2017 to Bolton College 2018. Each `type` (career / education / milestone in service, plus the dormant project and origin) carries icon + colour through one map (`accents.js`) applied only as accents — marker ring, connector gradient, date pill, card edge strip, tag hover — over the shared `custom-bg-abt` surface, which is what keeps five hues from reading as chaos; a `year`/`dateLabel` split lets a range-spanning role sit in its END year, where the story resolves, while printing its true span, and a data-file comment now pins the array's contract (grouped by year, monotonically descending — the era separators render on year CHANGE, so an interleaved year would mint a duplicate). **One trigger per node.** Marker ring draw (SVG `pathLength`), a one-shot ping, the connector's `scaleX` from the spine, and the card's staggered spring entrance all fire off the same `useInView` gated on `useLoaderRevealed` — LIVE, not latched, by later owner direction: leaving the viewport re-arms the node, so the full choreography replays every time a card comes on screen (the exit half of the toggle runs off-screen — a node only leaves `inView` once it is mostly out — so what the reader ever sees is entrances) (an IO fires behind the intro loader's z-9999 cover, so ungated entrances would play to nobody); cards counter-parallax ±18px over their on-screen life, and the hover lift lives on an inner wrapper so its CSS `transform` can never smear framer's scroll-linked writes (the issue #47 `transition` lesson). Reduced motion pins every entrance to its final state (mounted-gated `useReducedMotion`, the footer's SSR-agreement pattern), keeps the user-scrubbed spine fill, and drops comet, ping, pulse and parallax. **The ring slot.** The external GitHub hop is replaced IN PLACE by Journey (`Route` glyph) — `BtnList` stays at 8, so the sub-480 column slices and the 45° haptic detents are untouched, and GitHub keeps its footer ELSEWHERE terminal slot; the route is registered across the passage label map, the footer DEPARTURES board ("The road so far"), the 404 typo suggester, `FLUID_SCALE_PAGES` (four semantic `.journey-*` classes with `max()` legibility floors), and its own build-time OG/Twitter section cards. Mobile collapses to a left-rail spine with full-width cards behind `overflow-x-clip` on the section (cards awaiting entrance sit at x ±56, past a phone's edge — clip, not `hidden`, so no accidental scroll container; the fixed instruments sit outside the clip by construction). Verified against the dev server with agent-browser (Chrome CDP): fill, comet, odometer and rail tracked across a full 1440×900 scroll; the era-rail jump centres its era; both instruments measure `opacity: 0` over the footer; the serpentine/dial revision re-verified the same way — the snake passes all fifteen measured marker centres with 0.0px deviation at 1512×900, the dial swings 2026 → 2019 with the active year always horizontal on the needle and fully on-screen at both widths, a dial click on "Jump to 2023" centres that era and re-elects it, the re-centred pill sits exactly on the axis (756px at 1512, flush against the 390-wide rail), and lint stays clean; the instrument pass verified by live sampling — the rotor wound 1.17° → 3.68° → 24.59° → settling at 40.13° (continuous fractional angles, never 11.5° steps) across one smooth scroll, the draw-in's `stroke-dashoffset` held at the full 4665px arc length under the loader then swept to 0 in ~2.2s as it lifted, the dial renders exactly one silent-2020 graduation with 2021/2019 placed at −57.5°/−80.5°, and the `:has()` energise rule is present with `transition: transform` live on the markers; the small-screen echo verified at 390×844 — interactive nav `display: none`, the echo at z −10 / `pointer-events: none` / 0 buttons / 0 focusables / opacity 0.5, winding live (1.53° at the top → 54.43° after a 3000px scroll) — with the 1512 nav untouched (7 real buttons, echo hidden) and 820×1080 showing the dial and ghost year coexisting behind the cards; 390×844 shows zero horizontal overflow after the clip (436px→390px `scrollWidth`); the ring lists Journey where GitHub was and the Ember Passage carries the click; the manifest row reads "Now boarding" with `aria-current="page"`; `/jorney` suggests Journey first; the OG endpoint returns the seal card (200, `image/png`); `npm run lint` clean; production build prerenders `/journey` static at 6.72 kB page JS / 146 kB first load with all fifteen milestones. |

#### `/journey` — the overlap atlas: every role and course as parallel bars on one time axis, wired into the page's clock

| | |
|:--|:--|
| **Ref** | [#38](https://github.com/MA1002643/theabdullahfolio/issues/38) follow-up, owner-directed from a supplied concept page: port its "Overlap view" section — head, track chips, lane-packed Gantt grid and reading key — onto /journey between the page title and the timeline, restyled to the site's own palette and fully linked to the timeline and its clock. |
| **Files** | **new** `src/components/journey/TimelineAtlas.jsx` · `src/components/journey/index.jsx` (filter state + placement) · `src/components/journey/TimelineNode.jsx` (`dimmed` prop, `jn-<id>` jump anchors) · `src/app/data.js` (`journeyData` gains machine-readable `start`/`end` spans + `org` institution names) · `src/app/globals.css` (`.ja-*` type roles on the fluid-scale recipe, color-mix bar states) · `README.md` |
| **Details** | **The fifth instrument, and the only inline one.** Three lanes on the type-system accents (career ember `#ff6d05`, education blue `#60a5fa`, community amethyst `#fc83ff`), greedy interval packing so concurrent entries stack instead of colliding (a same-month handover still stacks — flush bars would erase the overlap the atlas exists to show), a month-true year axis, and a NOW line in the EraRail needle's ember closing every still-running bar's dashed edge. **A fixed-metric grid, on owner correction:** the scale is 40px/month — a 480px year gap — so the grid width follows the data (~4.5k px today), always out-measures the max-w-5xl section, pans on laptops exactly as on phones, and opens panned to the NOW edge to match the timeline's newest-first read. Every bar holds its full `org · role` caption INSIDE itself (second owner correction — no spilled or truncated text): at this scale every role of ~6+ months fits within its true span, and the three 4–5-month 2026 posts widen to a caption-width floor instead, since fitting the worst 45-character caption by scale alone would demand ~87px/month and a ~9,600px pan. Packing reserves max(duration, caption) per bar from the deliberately NON-fluid 11px mono metrics, so captions can never collide (verified: all fifteen captions inside their bars, zero overlaps, year gaps exactly 480px). **Instrument gating, on owner correction:** the clock dial and ghost year no longer ignite the moment any pixel of the list touches the viewport (which lit them during the atlas on the way down, and at the EST. end-cap on the way up from the footer) — an asymmetric useInView band (`-45% 0px -35% 0px`) holds them until the timeline reaches 45% down the viewport and cuts them once its tail clears the lower 35%, probed at five scroll landmarks in both directions. **Wired, not copied:** the container's one era election lights the elected era's bars exactly as it winds the clock dial and rolls the ghost year; selecting a bar jumps to its card with the clock's own scrollIntoView contract (auto under reduced motion) and hands it focus; the track chips dim non-matching bars AND timeline cards through one piece of state — a highlight, never a filter, so the spine geometry can't re-shuffle. **Hydration-safe by construction:** everything clock-derived (axis span, bar widths, NOW) mounts behind a null-initialised month state while the deterministic frame (packed lane rows, labels, chips) renders on the server — build month and visit month can differ without a mismatch. Verified live at desktop and phone widths: zero page overflow (the grid pans inside its own focusable region), clean console, jump/election/filter all exercised. |

| | |
|:--|:--|
| **Ref** | [#38](https://github.com/MA1002643/theabdullahfolio/issues/38) follow-up, owner-directed as a 16-point correction list: the page's greys and stray golds re-anchored onto the site's established colour voices, the atlas's time geometry made honest, the era labels made consistent across widths, and the footer Index halved in height. |
| **Files** | `src/components/journey/TimelineAtlas.jsx` · `src/components/journey/TimelineNode.jsx` · `src/components/journey/TimelineSpine.jsx` · `src/components/journey/EraRail.jsx` · `src/components/footer/FooterManifest.jsx` · `src/app/globals.css` |

The atlas's year rules become visible gold hairlines (they were `white/[0.06]`, invisible on the black ground), the ghost-year odometer sharpens to a crisp 1px `#ffaa2a` stroke with a wide low-alpha ember aura, tag hover now recolours the text together with the border, and the per-type accent strip is removed from the card's left edge so every card wears `/about`'s uniform gold frame (the type system still speaks through marker, connector and date pill). **A truthful NOW.** Open atlas bars closed one month cell past the NOW line, and the caption-width floor pushed the short 2026 roles into the 2027 label; they now clamp their right edge exactly on the line, and a still-running bar too short for its `org · role` prints the caption OUTSIDE, to the left of the bar — the lane packing reserves that room through a new footprint-start (`fs`), computed pessimistically against the last dated end so the SSR skeleton's lane counts stay deterministic. **No fire tail.** The comet's trailing streak is removed (at scroll 0 it hung above the NOW beacon and read as the beacon catching fire); the glowing head still rides the curve. **Era pills straddle the spine at every width.** The mobile anchor moves from the axis (`1.375rem`) to `0.5rem` — left of the serpentine's ±12px sway — so the line passes behind every pill exactly as md+'s centred straddle does (2021/2023 used to float clear of the line, sway-dependent). **Footer Index in two columns.** The six-row departures board re-cuts column-major 2×3 (About/Journey/Qualifications, then Projects/Contact/Guestbook), the status token stacked under each destination and the gate descriptors retired (hidden by rule, kept in the DOM), rows switching to uniform `border-bottom` so both columns close symmetrically, and the row count derived from `quickLinks.length` in the JSX so the list length stays free to change. |

#### `/journey` — the information layer: sticky lane labels, tenure readouts, the live line, the closing tally, and a ⌘K action set

| | |
|:--|:--|
| **Ref** | [#38](https://github.com/MA1002643/theabdullahfolio/issues/38) follow-up, owner-approved from a five-point enhancement pitch (all five taken): the page reads deeper without a single new effect — information density, not ornament. |
| **Files** | **new** `src/components/journey/JourneyPalette.jsx` · `src/components/journey/TimelineAtlas.jsx` · `src/components/journey/TimelineNode.jsx` · `src/components/journey/index.jsx` |
| **Details** | **Sticky lane labels.** The atlas opens panned to NOW, and Career/Education/Community lived only at the grid's far-left edge — every lane read nameless until you panned all the way back. The label column is now `position: sticky; left: 0` against the scroller (a flex item's containing scrollport), floating over the bars with a dark gradient scrim for legibility and `pointer-events-none` so a half-covered bar stays clickable through it. **Tenure readouts.** Every card prints its duration beside the date pill — the figure a recruiter would otherwise compute in their head. Months are inclusive of both endpoints (the LinkedIn/CV convention: Unisys MAY 2023 — SEP 2024 reads "1 yr 5 mo", the 17 months its own description claims); closed entries compute statically (SSR-deterministic), open entries render "N mo so far" mount-gated against the live clock, and the pill row gains `flex-wrap` so the widest pairing (Lidl's) drops the readout to its own line on narrow phones instead of squeezing the pill. **The live line.** One sentence under the atlas title — "Running today: 2 roles + 1 degree — in parallel." — states the page's whole thesis before the bars show it. Composed at module scope from `end: null` alone (a property of the record, not the clock), so it needs no mount gate. **The closing tally + the paper hand-off.** The EST. 2018 end-cap now carries the whole record in three figures ("8 YRS · 15 ENTRIES · 3 TRACKS" — all derived from the data, moving only when an entry is added) and the page's one outbound link: THE PAPER VERSION →, the same CV PDF the footer's Résumé link serves (`resumeUrl` imported from footer-data, never a second path) — the scroll story is the interactive CV, so it ends by offering the paper one. **⌘K.** `JourneyPalette` cuts `/journey`'s action set on the exact seam `GuestbookPalette` established (the shared palette component knows nothing about the page): Jump to <year> per era (reusing the EraRail clock's `era-<year>` anchor contract, reduced motion read via `matchMedia` at perform time), a jump to the overlap atlas (`id="journey-atlas"` on its root), Open the CV, and the standard Stone-Passage route navigation. |

|:--|:--|
| **Ref** | [#38](https://github.com/MA1002643/theabdullahfolio/issues/38) follow-up, owner-directed as a five-point correction list on the previous two passes. |
| **Files** | **new** `src/components/shared/FireText.jsx` · `src/components/journey/TimelineAtlas.jsx` · `src/components/journey/TimelineNode.jsx` · `src/components/journey/index.jsx` · `src/app/globals.css` |
