The size ladders move out of the markup into `--ring-size` (150/200-280/280/340, 220/300-400/400/460, 320/460-600/600/600, the 640-767 ramp included, all at their existing values) because a utility cannot be multiplied — the same move the laptop and the two type ladders already made. **Two real defects were found by testing rather than by reading.** The factor was first divided by `min(ladder, cap)` while CSS multiplies the ladder alone, so wherever the short-viewport cap was active the rendered ring came out larger than allowed — 1-6px of clearance instead of 16 at 900x700, 768x700, 1024x640 and 844x390. And a COLD LOAD at 1440x813 and 900x700 settled on a stale factor (0.739 against 0.686; 0.862 against 0.834) because the `ResizeObserver` watched only the laptop: at any height where the laptop is width-bound — most laptops — the headline re-flowing on webfont load, or the live maintenance header changing height when its data arrives, moves the base while the laptop's own box does not change a pixel, so nothing re-measured. The stage is observed too now, and `document.fonts.ready` re-measures, matching what the orbit already does. **Verified end to end.** 23 viewports from 1920x1080 to 667x375, sampling a whole 3s ripple at each so no phase is missed: **every ring closes inside the hero, none cut**, with clearance landing on the 16px gutter (16-21px) at every size where the fit binds and untouched (1.000) at 1920x1080, 1512x982, 1440x900, both tablets and every portrait phone. **No scrollbar on any of them** — 1440x790, 1366x768, 1280x800 and 1024x768 all back to `doc == viewport`, the page-growing reserve removed. Cold loads at six viewports settle on the final factor on first paint, with a forced re-measure changing nothing; repeated `resize` events show no creep and arriving at a size from above and from below gives the identical factor. The orbit sweep still reports zero off-screen cases, every hover label still fits, the SPA round trip returns the ring intact, and the production build and lint are clean |

#### The hero kept shrinking on a page that could now scroll instead, and the orbit's own tooltip was the one thing it was never sized around

| | |
|:--|:--|
| **Ref** | — (owner follow-up to the entry below, on the two conditions it reported as measured-but-unchanged: "for the above do whatever fixes you need to do") |
| **Files** | `src/app/globals.css` · `src/app/page.js` · `src/components/navigation/index.jsx` · `src/components/navigation/NavButton.jsx` · `README.md` |
| **Details** | **Two leftovers from the entry below, and the first is a premise that outlived its reason.** The hero's caps exist because the page could not scroll: a viewport too short for the composition had to have the composition shrunk into it, forever, with no lower bound. The page CAN scroll now, which makes unbounded shrinking first pointless and then harmful — at 844x390 the ramp had the laptop at **129px** and the orbit pinned to `MIN_SEMI_AXIS` with eight buttons piled on a 264px ellipse. Nothing was off-screen; it was simply crushed, and crushing had stopped being the only option. So the caps gain a floor. `--hero-h` (`max(100vh, var(--hero-min))`) is named once and every height-derived size on the page is re-expressed as a fraction of it — `73.7svh` -> `0.737 * var(--hero-h)` and the same for the title, role and lift ramps and `--hero-gap` — which is an identity above the floor and a hold below it, so the whole composition stops shrinking **together** instead of some parts freezing while others collapse. The shell's height moves there too (`min-height: var(--hero-h)`, no `min-h-screen` on the markup), so the page has one source for its height rather than a utility and an override. **`--hero-min` is 580px, and the number was swept rather than chosen.** Crowding was measured as the ring's tightest adjacent-button separation — eight buttons 45deg apart, so the tightest pair straddles the left/right turnaround at `2b*sin(22.5deg) = 0.765b` — expressed against the button's own width, where the desktop ring (1440x900, a=425) sits at **81%** and a landscape phone was getting ~30%. Swept at 480 / 540 / 580 / 620 / 660 / 700 against both that and the scroll it costs: 480 -> 43% at 844x390; **580 -> 74%**; 620 -> 78% but charges 1440x580 +40px, 1280x600 +20px and 1024x600 +20px of scroll; 700 -> +120px on a 1440x580. 580 is the LARGEST floor that costs nothing at any height that fits today — those three all still render without a scrollbar, `max(100vh, 580px)` being merely their own height — and it is also where the two narrowest landscape phones stop improving at all, the ring reaching its WIDTH cap at 667 and 736 wide (a=284, a=316) so height past 580 buys them nothing. Measured after: 844x390 goes a=132 -> **369** (the width cap, i.e. limited by the viewport exactly as a desktop is), laptop 129 -> 240px, and the page scrolls 190px to it. **The extra room is only usable because the ring stopped measuring the viewport.** `verticalSemiAxisLimit` derived its bound from `window.innerHeight - centreY`, which equals the room below the ring's centre only while the row's foot IS the viewport's foot — guaranteed under `h-screen`, false the moment the page can be taller than the screen, where it reads short and goes negative once the centre passes the fold. It now takes the orbit wrapper's own height, which is `h-1/2` of the hero row and centred on it and therefore exactly the centre-to-foot distance — verified empirically at three viewports as `rowH / 2` to within **0.005px**, with the wrapper's centre and the row's centre agreeing to 0.02px. The substitution is an identity wherever the page fits (ring centre 657.46 / 577.46 / 622.26 at 1440x900, 1440x740 and 768x900, unchanged) and correct where it does not. Its `Math.min` gains a real second operand in the process: the upper bound is now the distance to the top of the ROW — the headline's own edge — rather than to the top of the screen. **The second leftover is the hover label, and it moves rather than the ring.** `EDGE_GUTTER` reserves 16px under the lowest button; the label wants 23 (`top-full -mt-1 py-1` on a 20px line = a 28px box starting 1px inside the border), so hovering the bottom-most button clipped it at 1440x700, 1024x800, 768x700, 1440x620 and both landscape phones — 5-10px of the box and, at 1440x700, **4px of the glyphs themselves**. Buying that room out of the ring would cost `23 / ORBIT_TILT` = 113px of `a` wherever the height term binds, a 12% smaller ring everywhere to fix a tooltip, so instead the labels that cannot fit below flip above: one threshold (`labelFlipY = halfRow - halfButton - LABEL_DROP`) computed with the rest of the geometry, `bottom-full mb-1` as the exact reflection of `top-full -mt-1` so the text stays level with the circle's edge either way. It cannot flicker mid-hover because hovering already pauses the rotation. **Verified: every label now fits at all eight sizes measured, box and glyphs, where six of them clipped before** — and the fix is inert where it should be, flipping 0 of 8 labels at 1920x1080, 1440x900, 768x900 and 480x600 and only 1 or 2 (always the lowest buttons) where the room genuinely runs out. Labels were separately checked horizontally on the ring's left and right extremes at five widths down to 480 — the widest, "Qualifications", stays on screen everywhere. Re-swept for containment across 9 widths x 340-1100 tall: **zero off-screen cases**, the 16px gutter still exact, no scrollbar at any height from 580 up, real wheel scrolling reaches the ring on all three landscape phones, the SPA round trip returns the ring intact, and the production build and lint are clean |

#### The orbit hung off the bottom of the screen once its size floor bound, on a page with nowhere for it to go

| | |
|:--|:--|
| **Ref** | — (owner report: "at the moment the rings go out on some screen heights, the rings should never go out of the screen … the easiest solution that is coming to my mind is to apply scrolling to the page") |
| **Files** | `src/app/page.js` · `src/app/globals.css` · `README.md` |
| **Details** | **The orbit already sizes itself to fit, and that is exactly why the failure is invisible until it isn't.** `verticalSemiAxisLimit` solves the semi-axis out of the room below the ring's centre, so the ring cannot overflow — except that `a` is floored at `MIN_SEMI_AXIS` (132), and it has to be: without a floor a short enough viewport drives the ring to zero and stacks eight buttons on one point, which is worse than clipping and unrecoverable rather than merely ugly. Once the floor binds the ring has stopped shrinking, so anything still missing was paid out of the bottom of a shell that is `h-screen overflow-hidden` — not scrolled to, **gone**. Measured, lowest button's outer edge past the fold: **+28px at 1440x340 and 1024x340**, +16 at 1024x380, +11 at 768x340, +3-4 at 1440x420 and 1024x420, and clean from ~460px of height upward. That band is every landscape phone (a 390-tall device is ~340 after browser chrome) plus any window dragged under ~490px, and it is precisely the band the vertical-fit pass's fold-clearance sweep could not see: that sweep ran 375px and up in PORTRAIT, where the two-column layout has no orbit at all. **The fix is the owner's, and it is two halves that only work together.** The page may now grow past one screen — `h-screen` becomes `min-h-screen`, so the shell still fills the viewport at every height that fits and lengthens instead of amputating at the heights that do not — and `.hero-row` gains a `min-height` that is the room the navigation is guaranteed. That number is not a taste value, it is the component's own arithmetic read backwards: the orbit's wrapper is `h-1/2` of the row and centred on it, so the distance from the ring's centre to the foot of the row is exactly half the row's height, and the lowest button's edge sits `b + halfButton` below that centre with `b = MIN_SEMI_AXIS x ORBIT_TILT = 26.93px` once floored — hence `2 x (26.93 + halfButton + 16)`, giving the **144 / 152->160 / 160 / 168px** ladder that tracks `halfButtonWidth`'s own 29 / 33->37 / 37 / 41 rungs in `navigation/index.jsx`. Below 480px there is no orbit and the two-column nav needs *more*: four 40px buttons with `space-y-4`, centred 2vh above the row's middle, is `208px + 4vh`. The row is the last thing in the document, so room reserved there is room the page can always be scrolled to — the clipping is removed, not relocated one box outwards. **Three utilities on the shell are load-bearing and none of them is cosmetic.** `overflow-hidden` becomes `overflow-clip`: `hidden` makes the element a scroll container, so the growth would be swallowed into a nested scroller — the ring reachable only by scrolling INSIDE the hero, with no scrollbar to say so — where `clip` leaves the growth on the document, which is what actually scrolls. The clip stays on BOTH axes deliberately: the decoration overhangs this box in every direction (the backdrop's `scale(1.045)` overscan, the ripple rings spreading past the row) and none of it may lengthen the page — left visible on the vertical axis it added **20-25px of phantom scroll at every height**, 1125 against 1100. `w-screen` becomes `w-full` because `100vw` includes a scrollbar gutter this page can now have, which would sit the centred hero half a scrollbar off-centre. And `<main>` goes `h-full` -> `grow`, because `height: 100%` pins it to the shell and would put the row's reservation right back outside a fixed box. One further trap, worth its own line: Tailwind's `flex-1` is `flex: 1 1 0%`, and a PERCENTAGE basis against an indefinite height falls back to `content` — so during intrinsic sizing the row reported the laptop's *uncapped* intrinsic height (~507px with its lift) even though `max-height: 100%` shrinks it in real layout, which was the true source of that phantom scroll. `flex-basis: 0px` is definite; identical in real layout, honest when asked. **Verified end to end.** Ellipse geometry recovered EXACTLY rather than sampled — buttons 0 and 2 are 90deg apart, so `a = hypot(x0, x2)` whatever phase the lap is in, where `max abs(x)` under-reports by up to 7.6% and would have hidden a near miss. Swept 1920 / 1440 / 1366 / 1024 / 900 / 768 / 640 / 500 / 480 / 479 / 430 / 360 / 320 wide x 340-1100 tall: **zero off-screen cases**, with the 16px `EDGE_GUTTER` honoured exactly (16-17px of slack) in the floored regime. **Nothing scrolls at 500px of height and up** — `scrollHeight` equals the viewport at every one — and the ring's centre and drop come back byte-identical to the pre-change sweep at every height that already fit (1440x900 cY 657 drop 64, 1440x740 cY 577 drop 19, 1024x820 cY 617 drop 59, 1440x500 cY 411). The two-column nav is contained at 320x480 through 479x900, and the 44 real-device sizes in the matrix scroll at exactly three: 932x430, 844x390, 667x375. Production build and lint clean, all four sub-routes still 200. Two pre-existing conditions were re-measured and are unchanged rather than introduced: the ring at 844x390 is still at its floor and still crowds the laptop (now fully visible instead of clipped), and the bottom-most button's hover label still overhangs the page foot by 4-7px at 1440x700 / 1024x800 / 768x700, the ring keeping a 16px gutter against a ~24px label. The homepage's `prefers-reduced-motion` hydration error is likewise pre-existing — confirmed by building HEAD without this change into an APFS clone and serving it side by side, where it throws the same pair as minified React #418 / #423 |

#### The orbit never went behind the laptop, outgrew the screen on small tablets, and stood up three times on the way to a desktop

| | |
|:--|:--|
| **Ref** | — (owner report, three parts: "when the navigation buttons orbit around the laptop the navigation buttons go on top of the laptop when they should be going behind the laptop"; "between page width 480px and 639px the orbit is so big that icons on both sides go off the screen — regardless of the screen size the orbit should never exceed the screen width"; "regardless of screen size the orbit tilt should be the same on all screen sizes … the correct tilt is already implemented between 480px and 639px, the tilt at 640px changes, then at 768px again, then at 1024 again") |
| **Files** | `src/components/navigation/index.jsx` · `src/components/navigation/NavButton.jsx` · `README.md` |
| **Details** | **(1) The ring could not pass behind the laptop, and the reason is the same stacking-context trap that had it buried before — inverted.** The previous pass gave the wrapper `z-30` to lift it off the laptop's `z-20`; but `position` + *any* `z-index` makes an element its own stacking context, and a stacking context collapses everything inside it to **one** depth against the rest of the page. So the ring could only ever be wholly in front (`z-30`) or wholly behind (the `z-0` before it) — the `z-50` on each button never reached the laptop at all. An orbit needs neither: it needs the ring to be able to pass on **both** sides of the art. The wrapper is now left at `z-index: auto`, which makes it transparent to stacking so each button's own z-index competes with the laptop's inside the hero row (`relative z-10`), and the depth is computed per frame from the sign of the button's own `y`: the top of the ellipse is the half of the ring running away from the viewer, so `y < 0` paints at **10** (behind the laptop's 20) and `y >= 0` at **40** (in front). Both values still clear the ripple rings and the contact shadow, so the ring never falls behind the ground effects — only behind the laptop, and only for the half of each lap it spends back there. The hero row is a *sibling* of the live maintenance header (`z-50`) under `<main>`, so the header still paints over everything here whatever the buttons ask for. The same rule sorts the two buttons meeting at the ellipse's left/right turnaround against each other for free: the one arriving on the near side passes over the one leaving on the far side. **(2) The ring was sized in fixed pixels against a viewport it had never measured.** Below 640px the semi-axis was a constant 264 (<500px) or 297 (500-639px) — drawn on a half-viewport of 240px at 480 and 250px at 500 — so the outermost buttons hung **53px off the left edge at 480, 75px at 500, 23px at 600**, clipped by `main`'s `overflow-x-hidden` rather than scrolled to. It is now derived, once, for every width: `a = min(425, width/2 − halfButtonWidth − 16)`. Sizing runs to the button's **outer edge**, not to the path its centre travels, which is the distinction the old constants missed — a centre path that fits still lets 29-41px of art hang off the screen. Because `a` and `halfButtonWidth` step in opposite directions by the same 4px at 640/768/1024, the ring's outer envelope comes out **continuous across those breakpoints**: measured, the outermost button's edge sits at exactly **16.0px** from both viewport edges at every width from 480 to 900, then pulls inward (49.5 / 46 / 174 / 254 / 494 / 814px) as the 425 cap takes over from ~948px up, so nothing on desktop moves by a pixel. **(3) The tilt is one constant now, and it is the one the owner picked.** What the eye reads as the ring's tilt is the ellipse's axis ratio, and every band had chosen its own: measured live, **0.208** below 500px, **0.204** at 500-639, then **0.783** at 640, **0.716** at 700, **0.656** at 767, **0.735** at 768x1024, **0.556** at 900 and **0.480** at 1024 and up — the ring visibly standing up three times between a phone and a desktop. `ORBIT_TILT = 0.204` is the 480-639px band's own ratio (297 x 60.5) and now applies everywhere: **0.2040 at all 17 viewports measured**. It is also `cos(78.2°)`, which puts the orbit on the same plane the ripple rings under the laptop are already drawn on (`perspective(600px) rotateX(80deg)`), so ring and ground finally agree about where the floor is. **Viewport height drops out of the sizing entirely** — it was only ever an input because the ring stood nearly upright on tablets (b up to 233), the very tilt this removes — and that flattening independently fixes a vertical overflow nobody had reported: at 1024x800 the old ellipse put the lowest button **37px below the bottom of the viewport**, where it now clears by 76px (114-401px everywhere else measured). The `screenSize` band state goes with the bands, replaced by the one boolean the render actually consumed (`isColumnLayout`); the sub-480px two-column layout is untouched. **Verified in the browser, not by reading the numbers.** Depth: 17 viewports (480 → 2560, every breakpoint edge and both sides of it) with the ring parked at its reduced-motion resting angles — at every one, the far-side button reports `z-index: 10` and a real hit test (`elementsFromPoint` at its centre) returns **the laptop above it**, while the near-side button reports 40 and comes back on top; then 6s of live rotation at 1440x900, **349 rAF frames x 8 buttons = 2,792 samples, 0 depth-rule violations**, across **1,134 frames of a button inside the laptop's box** — 562 far-side samples with the laptop painting over the button and 563 near-side with the button over the laptop. Fit: 0 overflow at all 17 viewports, and no horizontal document overflow at 360/400/479/480/1440. Layout swap across the 480px boundary exercised live in both directions (900 → 470 → 900 → 400 → 600), 8 buttons every time. **The one trade, stated plainly:** a flat ellipse packs the two buttons that meet at its left/right turnaround closer, because they share an *x* there and are separated only by `2b·sin(22.5°) = 0.765b`. Measured at the worst frame, adjacent centres close to **30.4px against 58px buttons at 480** (was 42.1), **39.8px at 600** (was 45.9), and **66.4px against 82px buttons at 1440** (was 156px — desktop had no turnaround overlap before and now has ~19%). This is the geometry of the tilt that was chosen rather than a defect in it: it is the same overlap the 480-639px reference band has always had, and the new depth sort is what keeps it legible — the pair reads as one button passing in front of another, which is what it is. ESLint clean; the only console output on the route is the pre-existing `/api/spotify` 502 and the pre-existing reduced-motion hydration mismatch in the loader's `EmblemSeal` |

#### The 640–767px hero sat still for 128px and then jumped a third — with the laptop the only thing still growing through it

| | |
|:--|:--|
| **Ref** | — (owner report: "between width 640px and 767px the size of navigation buttons, the size of the icons, the size of the laptop, title and subtitle they get too small which look unprofessional") |
| **Files** | `src/app/globals.css` · `src/app/page.js` · `src/components/navigation/index.jsx` · `README.md` |
| **Details** | **The band is a plateau, and the report is about what it is measured against.** Tailwind's `sm` spans 128px on this page and every hero size is frozen at its 640px value across all of it, then steps 33–67% at `md`. Measured at 900px tall before the change — button **56 / 64 / 64 / 72**, icon **24 / 24 / 24 / 40**, title **41.6 / 48 / 48 / 64**, role **16 / 19.2 / 19.2 / 22.4**, laptop **450 / 539 / 584 / 358** at 639 / 700 / 767 / 768. Two faults compound, and the second is what makes the first visible. The **icon never left the phone**: `w-6` has no `sm:` step, so a 24px glyph is dropped into a circle that *did* grow to 64px — 37% of the button against 55% at `md` and 43% below 640 — and since the button is the only part of the control that scales, the ring reads as eight empty discs with something small in the middle of them. The **laptop is a percentage** (`sm:w-[75%]`) where every neighbour is a step, so it is the one element still growing through the band: 76% of the viewport at 767, wide enough to swallow the orbit ring, before snapping to a fixed `22rem` at 768 and losing **226px in one pixel of width**. That is why the band reads as *shrunken* rather than merely small — type and controls held still underneath the one thing still inflating, which is the reference the eye judges them against. Worth stating plainly since the report named it: the laptop measures **larger** in this band than anywhere else on the site, and reining it in (owner-confirmed before building) is what makes everything around it read as correctly sized. **The band is interpolated, not re-stepped.** `calc(A + (B − A) × (100vw − 640px) / 128)` is a straight line across the 128px the band spans — number × length ÷ number is a length, so no unitless-ratio trick is needed for a single bounded band, in contrast to the `tan(atan2())` the `--fluid-scale` system next to it needs for a factor it multiplies many properties by. Every **A** is the value the band already had at 640 and every **B** is the **real `md` value**, so each ramp arrives at the exact number Tailwind's `md:` utility takes over with: the cliff does not shrink, it stops existing. Twelve ramps — title 48→64, role 19.2→22.4, laptop 450→352 and its margin 24→96, contact shadow 383→300 / 66→52, the three ripple rings 200→280 / 300→400 / 460→600, button 64→72 with its padding 16→12, and icon 28→40. Three are corrections rather than bridges. The **icon starts at 28, not the 24 it inherits**, because what the eye reads is the glyph's share of the disc, and 24-in-64 is a *drop* from the phone's 24-in-56 (0.375 against 0.429) — 28/64 is 0.4375, the phone's proportion continued, and the ramp carries it to `md`'s 0.556 from the inside. The **contact shadow** is re-derived from the ratio `md` and `lg` both keep (300/352 = 0.85, 420/480 = 0.88); the band's flat 210px was 0.36 of a 584px laptop, the same laptop-in-%-shadow-in-px divergence the portrait-phone pass fixed with `63vw`, and without it the shadow would have jumped a third wider at 768 while the laptop it belongs to held still. The **laptop's `mb-6`→`md:mb-24`** is ramped because the hero row centres the laptop-plus-rings cluster, so that step moved the laptop 32px upward at 768 — invisible while the same boundary was also taking 226px off its width, but with the width now continuous it was the only thing left moving, which promotes it from a detail to *the* artifact. **Specificity, not source order, is what makes these hold, and that is the finding worth keeping.** These rules sit thousands of lines below `@tailwind utilities`, but Tailwind emits JIT **arbitrary-value** utilities at the end of the sheet — measured in the built `layout.css`, `md:w-[22rem]` lands at byte **279830** against this block's **238859** — so a bare `.laptop` loses to `md:w-[22rem]` on order, while `.home-shell` two rules earlier beats `h-screen` on order because `h-screen` is a *core* utility emitted up at line 3. Written bare first, the block silently no-op'd on **seven of its nine** rules; only the two carrying a combinator (`.nav-button > span`, `.nav-button svg`, both 0,1,1) took effect. Every selector is now element-qualified (`img.laptop`, `h1.home-title`, `div.borderline`) for the same 0,1,1. `h1.home-title` is a **new hook added for this reason alone**: `.text-glow-stroke-neon` is shared with the ABOUT ME / CONTACT ME sub-page headers ([#104](https://github.com/MA1002643/theabdullahfolio/issues/104)), so sizing it here would have resized those too — the same reasoning `.home-shell` exists on. The orbit reads the button ramp as well: `halfButtonWidth` now interpolates **33 → 37** across the band instead of standing at 33, hitting continuously the pair of constants it used to step between, so the ellipse stays sized around the button's real outer edge and the envelope identity from [#93](https://github.com/MA1002643/theabdullahfolio/issues/93) is unaffected — it is a subtraction, not a property of the breakpoints. **Verified on computed style at 639 / 640 / 700 / 767 / 768 / 1024**, all twelve ramps exact at both endpoints (at 700, t = 0.469: button 67.75, pad 14.125, icon 33.625, laptop 404.06, shadow 344.09/59.44, rings 237.5/346.88/525.63, title 55.5, role 20.7 — every one matching its formula to the rounding). At **767 against 768**: button 71.94/72, icon 39.91/40, laptop 352.77/352, shadow 300.65/300 and 52.11/52, rings 279.4/399.2/598.9 against 280/400/600, title 63.88/64, role 22.38/22.4 — all within ~1px, the remainder being the float animation's own 1.02 scale sampled at different phases. Nothing outside the band moved: 639 returns every original value and 1024 returns every `lg` value exactly (button 80, icon 48, laptop 480, mb 96, shadow 420/64, rings 340/460/600, title 80, role 25.6). The ring's outer envelope holds its 16px gutter throughout (leftmost button edge at exactly 16.0px, no horizontal document overflow), and the 640 boundary gains no new step beyond the icon's deliberate 4px, which is what keeps the *ratio* from going backwards there. All five targeted class names are home-only — `Navigation` is imported by `app/page.js` and nothing else, and the xs-mobile column button that shares `.nav-button` renders only below 480px, which this query cannot reach. Only console output on the route is the pre-existing `/api/spotify` 502 |

#### The ripple rings and the contact shadow were centred on the stage, not on the laptop's base

| | |
|:--|:--|
| **Ref** | — (owner report: "the rings under the laptop at any given screen size/width they always should be starting from under the laptop — that is not happening at the moment; the center point should be the center point of the laptop base") |
| **Files** | `src/app/page.js` · `src/app/globals.css` · `README.md` |
| **Details** | **The ground effects are the only thing telling the eye where the laptop meets the causeway, and they were tracking the wrong box.** All four — three ripple rings and the contact shadow — were `absolute` with no offsets, which centres them on the STAGE, plus a flat `mt-16`. The stage is not the laptop: `--laptop-w` is a four-rung ladder, the `max-height` bound trims it further on short viewports, and `safe center` moves the artwork between centred and top-aligned inside that box. Every one of those moves the laptop and leaves the rings where they were. **Two errors, and only one of them was vertical.** The drift down the viewport was the visible one. The other had always been there: the laptop is drawn TURNED, so its base does not centre on the middle of its own bounding box — measured off `laptop.png`'s alpha channel, the art runs edge to edge (no transparent padding; last solid row 545 of 548) and the silhouette below the widest row is the deck's ground footprint in perspective, spanning x 68-783 at y=432 and narrowing to the front corner at (349, 545). Area-weighted, that footprint centres on **(0.52, 0.85)** of the image box — so the rings had been half a percent of the laptop's width off-centre at every viewport the site has ever had, and the error grew with the artwork. Those two fractions are now named constants with the measurement recorded beside them. **Measured in JS, deliberately.** The laptop's box moves for three reasons CSS cannot hand to a sibling (ladder, height bound, alignment switch), so `app/page.js` reads `offsetLeft/Top/Width/Height` off the laptop and publishes `--lap-base-x` / `--lap-base-y` on the stage; the four ground elements position from those. `offset*` and NOT `getBoundingClientRect`: the laptop carries a permanent float animation (`translateY` + `scale(1.02)`), and a rect would fold that in and make the rings breathe with the laptop — the exact opposite of what a contact shadow is for. A `ResizeObserver` on the laptop itself covers every way the box can change, including the webfont landing and re-flowing the headline above it, without this component needing to know which happened. The CSS defaults reproduce the old placement exactly, so the pre-measurement frame is never worse than what it replaces. **One trap**: the centring uses the independent `translate` property, not `transform: translate(...)`. These rings are mid-animation almost all the time — `animate-ripple-neon` drives `transform` through `perspective(600px) rotateX(80deg) scale(...)` — and an animation beats an inline transform on the property it animates, so a `transform`-based centring would simply be dropped for the whole ripple. `translate` composes BEFORE `transform`, so the ring is centred on the anchor and then tilted about its own centre; the two never contend. **Verified end to end: 132 grid viewports (12 widths x 11 heights, 360-2560 by 375-1200), every ring and the shadow anchored to the laptop's measured base with a worst-case error of 0.50px**, across rendered laptop widths from 117 to 480. Checked against untransformed layout (`offsetLeft/Top`) rather than bounding rects on purpose — a `rotateX(80deg)` element under `perspective` projects its far edge shorter than its near one, so its rect centre is NOT its own centre, and measuring that way reports a phantom 3-56px error that grows with ring size and animation phase. Everything else re-confirmed unchanged in the same run: **0 plate/text overlaps** (minimum gaps 19px and 35px), **0 laptop clipping**, **0 horizontal overflow**, and the orbit's depth sort still splitting 4 behind / 4 in front at every viewport that has an orbit — the 22 cells reporting otherwise are exactly the 2 widths x 11 heights below 480px, which run the two-column layout and have no ring to sort. ESLint clean |

#### The orbit ran across the middle of the laptop instead of around its base

| | |
|:--|:--|
| **Ref** | — (owner report: "the navigation buttons need to be moved a bit down too … at the moment they are vertically almost in the middle of the laptop") |
| **Files** | `src/components/navigation/index.jsx` · `README.md` |
| **Details** | **The tilt was on the ground plane; the ring's HEIGHT never was.** `ORBIT_TILT` is `cos(78.2deg)` precisely so the orbit lies in the plane the three ripple rings are drawn on — but the ring was still *centred* on the hero row, which is very nearly the laptop's own centre, so the buttons cut across the artwork rather than circling its foot. Measured at 1440x790: ring centre **602** against a laptop centre of **600**, while the ripple rings — the floor the laptop stands on — sat at **646 / 657 / 680**. The orbit and the ground rings agreed about the plane's ANGLE and disagreed about its HEIGHT, which is the half of that agreement that was missing. `ORBIT_DROP = 64` closes it, and it is not a taste value: it is the `mt-16` the three ripple rings already carry in `app/page.js`, so the orbit and the ground rings are now offset from the same box by the same amount. **It is a ceiling, not a fixed offset.** Dropping the ring spends room at the bottom, and a landscape phone has none — so the drop is computed as whatever is left over *after* the ring's full width has been paid for (`vh - (b + halfButton + gutter) - centre`, clamped to 0..64), which means the ring's SIZE is never traded for its position. Tall viewports take all 64; 736x414, where the lowest button already cleared the fold by 17px, takes 13; 926x428 takes 0 and keeps its ring intact. Measured drop of the ring's centre below the laptop's: 48px at 1440x790, 72 at 1440x820, 113-116 from 1512x982 up. Ring centre against the middle ripple ring afterwards: **within 4-8px at 1440x790 / 820, 1512x982 and 1680x1050**, against 42-55px of divergence before. **One trap, and it would have been silent.** The rendered `y` is now `b·sin(t) + drop`, but the per-frame depth sort reads the sign of `y` to decide whether a button is on the far half of the lap (behind the laptop, z 10) or the near half (in front, z 40). Adding the drop before that test biases the sign, and at any drop greater than `b` it parks **every** button on the near side — the ring would simply stop passing behind the laptop, which is the whole effect [#93](https://github.com/MA1002643/theabdullahfolio/issues/93) exists to produce, and nothing about the layout would have looked wrong. The orbit's own `yOrbit` is therefore kept separate and the depth test reads that; verified at 1440x900, the eight buttons still split **4 behind / 4 in front**. **Verified: 132 grid viewports (360-2560 x 375-1200)** — depth split intact, no horizontal overflow, the laptop still never clipped, and headline collisions checked by real rectangle intersection rather than by vertical band: **2 of 132 cells put a button on the role line, both at 360px wide, which is the sub-480 two-column layout and not the orbit at all** (that layout is fixed-position and already documented as running past the fold below ~420px of height). ESLint clean |

#### The laptop sat on the role line's banknote plate, and on the word ENGINEER, at 12 of 18 viewports

| | |
|:--|:--|
| **Ref** | — (owner report, on a 13" MacBook Pro M1: "the laptop overlapping with the banknote lines and the Engineer text embedded into the banknote. also more than half of the main software engineer text. That should never be happening regardless of the screen height, width — there should be a noticeable gap at all times between the entire Software Engineer text section and the actual laptop") |
| **Files** | `src/app/globals.css` · `src/app/page.js` · `README.md` |
| **Details** | **Swept before touching anything, and it was not one viewport.** The plate was overlapped at **12 of 18** sizes measured and the printed TEXT at four of them: 1440x820 plate -11, **1440x790 plate -15**, 1440x760 -14, 1440x741 -11, 1440x700 plate -40 and text -13, 1280x800 -6, 1024x768 -8, 1024x600 plate -36 text -12, 736x414 -19/-3, 926x428 -18/-1, 844x390 -15/0, 667x375 -14/+1. A 13" MacBook Pro is 1440x900 scaled, which lands at **790-820 of viewport** once browser chrome is taken — the deepest part of the hole, which is why it was reported from there. **Two causes, and neither was a number that wanted tuning.** (1) **The plate is not in the layout.** `.hero-role-ground` is an absolutely positioned canvas and is deliberately BIGGER than the heading it backs — measured at 1440x900 it spans 293-386 where the type spans 323-358, overhanging ~30px above the ink and ~28px below, which is `PAD_TOP_EM` / `PAD_BOTTOM_EM` (1.225em each) by construction. The upper overhang was already reserved by `.hero-role`'s `margin-top: 1.3em`. The lower one was reserved by an ASSUMPTION, written into that same note at the time: *"the plate's matching bottom overhang costs nothing because the next thing down is the laptop's own empty half of the hero."* That is precisely the assumption that fails once the hero is short, because the laptop's empty half is the first thing the layout spends. It is now reserved symmetrically, `margin-bottom: 1.3em`, in the same em so it tracks the plate at every size for the same reason the top one does. (2) **The cluster overflowed UPWARD.** The hero row is `flex-1` with `align-items: center`, and `flex-1` carries `min-height: auto` — so when the laptop plus its lift exceeded the row, the row refused to shrink and the centred cluster spilled EQUALLY IN BOTH DIRECTIONS. Both earlier passes on this hero checked only the bottom fold; the upward half of that same spill is what was landing on the type, which is why a defect this wide survived two rounds of measurement. **The fix is a structural bound, not another `svh` ramp.** The laptop is now sized `width: auto` + `max-width` + `max-height` — the one configuration in which CSS shrinks a replaced element to satisfy BOTH limits while preserving its aspect ratio (CSS 2.1 s10.4); with a definite `width` a `max-height` squashes instead, and with `object-fit: contain` it letterboxes inside a box that never shrinks. `max-height` is a percentage of `.hero-stage`, the stage is `height: 100%` of the row, and the row now carries `min-height: 0` at EVERY height (not just short ones — the collision starts around 870px of viewport, far above the short end the previous pass was scoped to) plus `--hero-gap` as `padding-block`. So the laptop physically cannot be taller than the space between the headline and the fold, whatever the viewport does. **Revised once on review, and the revision is the interesting half.** The first cut wrote that bound as `calc(100% - var(--laptop-lift))` and the owner rejected the result: "I don't want you to reduce the size of the laptop … just shift everything a bit down". They were right, and the arithmetic was the giveaway — the lift is `margin-bottom`, i.e. EMPTY SPACE below the artwork, so holding height back for it shrank the visible laptop to reserve room for nothing at all. At 1440x790 that cost **148px of laptop, 332 against a full 480**. The bound is now a plain `max-height: 100%` and the lift is simply allowed to overflow the bottom of the stage, where it is invisible. What stops that overflow being taken out of the TOP instead — which is the original bug — is one keyword: `align-items: safe center` on the stage. Plain `center` centres the laptop's OUTER box, lift included, so it lifts the artwork half a lift above the box's centre and back onto the plate; `safe` keeps that centring while the outer box fits and falls back to START alignment the moment it does not, so the spill can only leave downward. `flex-start` would have guaranteed the same gap but would also have pinned the laptop to the top of the row at TALL viewports, moving it ~48px off the ring's centre on a desktop that was never broken. Net effect at the reported viewport: **the laptop is 467px instead of 332, and the gap is 53px instead of -15**. `--hero-cap` survives only to keep the RINGS in proportion, no longer to prevent a collision it can no longer cause. The width ladder moved to `--laptop-w` because `width` had to become `auto`; same four values, applied as `max-width`. **One trap worth recording**: a `--laptop-w: 75%` rung looked obviously right for the `sm` band and would have silently killed the 640-767 ramp, because the ladder sits LATER in the file than the dead-band block and `sm:w-[75%]` was only ever reachable in exactly that range. It is deliberately absent, with a comment saying why. **A second trap cost a false failure**: the first sweep reported a 5px minimum plate gap, which is the plate's ResizeObserver — coalesced at 120ms — still holding the PREVIOUS viewport's canvas when probed 110ms after a resize. Re-measured at 400ms the same viewport reads a 1.10em overhang (inside the 1.3em reserved) and a 38px gap. Every number below is taken at a 430ms settle. **Verified end to end: 132 grid viewports (12 widths x 11 heights, 360-2560 by 375-1200) with ZERO overlaps of either the plate or the text and the laptop never once clipped at the bottom — minimum plate gap 19px, minimum text gap 35px** — plus **28 real device and window sizes, also zero failures**, including every 13" MacBook Pro configuration from the report, now at full laptop size: 1440x900 fullscreen (laptop 490, plate gap 49), 1440x860 (493 / 46), 1440x820 (481 / 54, was 372 / -11), **1440x790 (467 / 53, was 332 / -15)**, 1440x760 (432 / 47, was 292 / -14) and 1440x700 (376 / 42, was 245 / -40) — the laptop widths reading a little over their 480 base because the float animation's 1.02 scale is sampled mid-cycle. Also re-checked that the sub-480 two-column layout, `md` and `lg` are untouched where height is not binding. **Two things this does not fix, stated plainly.** The `min-width: 480px` guard came OFF the vertical-fit block in the same change — it had been starving narrow-and-short viewports rather than protecting them, collapsing the laptop to **13px wide** at 360x375 because the headline was never capped there — but that combination still renders a 47-66px laptop, and the sub-480 two-column nav still runs past the fold at heights under ~420. And 20 of the 120 grid cells still clip the ring at the bottom by 1-18px, all of them at 375 or 414px of height paired with widths of 768 and up: that is the documented `MIN_SEMI_AXIS` floor preferring a little clipping to stacking eight buttons on one point, and no real device pairs those dimensions — all five landscape phones tested (667x375, 736x414, 844x390, 926x428, 932x430) clear the fold. ESLint clean |

#### The hero was sized only by width, on a page that cannot scroll — so a short viewport lost the orbit and half the laptop below the fold

| | |
|:--|:--|
| **Ref** | — (owner-directed follow-up to the dead-band pass above, which surfaced this while measuring: "yes do that pass too") |
| **Files** | `src/app/globals.css` · `src/app/page.js` · `src/components/navigation/index.jsx` · `README.md` |
| **Details** | **Every size in this hero came from WIDTH, and the shell is `h-screen overflow-hidden` — so anything that did not fit was not scrolled to, it was gone.** Measured overflow of the lowest interactive element past the fold: **+189px at 667x375**, +135 at 736x414, +123 at 1024x600, +19 at 1440x700, first fitting at ~730px of height (1440x760 clears by 36). So this was never a phone bug: it is landscape phones, **1366x768 laptops** (~640px of viewport once browser chrome is taken), and any desktop window dragged shorter than ~730px. On a 375-tall phone the header and headline alone took **71% of the screen** before the laptop asked for another 300px. **The row could not absorb it either**: `flex-1` carries `min-height: auto`, so the hero row refused to shrink below its content and grew DOWNWARD off-screen — measured at 667x375 it wanted 206px inside an available 163, which put its centre 22px below the space it was given and had the whole orbit computed around that displaced centre. **The correction is continuous**, not a compact mode that snaps on, for the same reason the band above is a ramp: a second cliff is not a fix for the first. Mechanism is chosen per element by what CSS actually allows, and the split is the interesting part. **Boxes take `max-width` / `max-height`** — the decisive property of a cap is that it needs no knowledge of the value it is capping, so all four width bands and every dead-band ramp keep their existing rules untouched and simply stop at a ceiling; the laptop is `height: auto` from Tailwind preflight, so capping its width shrinks it along the aspect ratio with no distortion, and since it is the only NON-absolutely-positioned child of the stage it is also the only one contributing to the row's height. **Type and margins cannot**: there is no `max-font-size`, and `1em` inside a `font-size` resolves against the PARENT rather than the element, so CSS offers no way to say "whatever you already are, but no larger". `min()` is the only tool and it needs the width-derived value as an operand — which is why the headline's four breakpoint values, its 640-767 ramp, and the laptop's `mb-6` / `md:mb-24` now live in `--title-w`, `--role-w` and `--laptop-mb` ladders in `globals.css`. **The numbers are identical** (2.6 / 3→4 / 4 / 5rem, 1 / 1.2→1.4 / 1.4 / 1.6rem, 24 / 24→96 / 96px); the corresponding utilities were deleted from the markup in the same change so there is exactly one source rather than two copies of one ladder, and 390x844 was re-measured afterwards to confirm the base rung still returns 41.6 / 16 / 273 / 24 exactly. **One tuned ramp drives the whole stage.** `--hero-cap` runs 480px at 740 tall to 211px at 375, and the rings, contact shadow and laptop are expressed as their `lg` FRACTIONS of it (280/480, 400/480, 600/480, 420/480, 64/480) rather than as five separately-tuned ramps — which buys two properties worth having: at the non-binding end every cap reaches its own `lg` value at the same moment, and wherever two caps both bind the composition holds the `lg` proportion exactly, because both sides came off one number. The 211 endpoint is the vertical budget solved rather than a taste call: at 375 the header takes 81 and the capped headline 131, leaving 163 for the entire hero row, which the laptop (147) plus its capped lift (16) exactly fills. **The orbit needed a height term of its own**, and this is where the #93 conclusion needed amending. That pass recorded "height no longer enters into it" on the strength of the lowest button clearing the foot of the viewport by 76-176px everywhere measured — but that set topped out at 1024x800 and contained no short viewport, and at 667x375 the RING is the binding element, not the laptop. `ORBIT_TILT` is owner-specified and identical at every size, so the tilt is not available as a lever: a ring that must occupy less vertical space has to get narrower and flatten in proportion, which is exactly what capping `a` does since `b` is derived from it. `a` therefore takes a third limit beside the width term and the 425 cap. That limit is **measured, not predicted** — the ring's centre is read off its own live wrapper rather than computed from header and headline heights, which change with the viewport, change again when the webfont lands, and belong to markup this component does not own; a hard-coded fraction of the viewport would have been a copy of somebody else's number. There is no feedback loop in reading it (the wrapper is `h-1/2 w-full` with absolutely-positioned buttons, so `a` cannot move the box being measured), and two extra re-measures close the gaps: `document.fonts.ready`, because a fallback face has different metrics and moves the row after the effect has already run; and an effect on `isColumnLayout`, because on the crossing UP out of the two-column layout the wrapper does not exist yet — the render that creates it is the one the state change triggers, so the `resize` pass that flipped the flag measured a null ref. A `MIN_SEMI_AXIS` floor applies to the height term **alone**, never to the width term, so an absurdly short viewport accepts a little clipping instead of collapsing eight buttons onto one point — verified at a deliberately unreal 900x300, where the ring holds 24px between adjacent centres and clips 19px rather than stacking. **Verified across 12 heights** (375 / 390 / 400 / 414 / 428 / 500 / 600 / 700 / 740 / 760 / 900 / 1024) and the widths that pair with them: every short viewport now clears the fold, by 12px at 667x375, 16 at 736x414, 18 at 760x400 and 31 at 620x500, with no horizontal document overflow at any of them. Nothing tall regressed — 1440x900 returns all eight `lg` values exactly (title 80, role 25.6, laptop 480, mb 96, shadow 420/64, rings 340/460/600, button 80, icon 48), 768x1024 all five `md` values, and 390x844 the base rung with the two-column layout intact. At exactly **740px tall the block is active but inert**: every cap evaluates to its own `lg` value (479.98 / 96 / 279.97 / 399.96 / 599.97), so it switches on with nothing to do and grows teeth continuously as the viewport shortens. The dead-band work above was re-measured at 700x900 after the ladder refactor — all ten ramps still exact. The one trade, stated plainly: because the tilt is fixed, a ring that fits a short viewport vertically is also narrower horizontally, so at 736x414 it sits 132px inside each screen edge instead of the usual 16 — a compact orbit rather than a full-width one. Turnaround crowding is nonetheless BETTER than the case #93 already accepts: **48px between adjacent centres against 68px buttons (30%) at 667x375**, against 30.4px against 58px (48%) at 480 wide. ESLint clean; only console output on the route is the pre-existing `/api/spotify` 502 |

#### Nav glyphs sat 4px high in their own buttons at every size but `lg`

| | |
|:--|:--|
| **Ref** | — (owner report: "the icons inside the navigation buttons need to be centered at all times; at the moment that is not the case on some of the screen sizes") |
| **Files** | `src/components/navigation/NavButton.jsx` |
| **Details** | The orbital button laid its glyph and its hover label out as two in-flow siblings of a `flex flex-col items-center` box, which is `justify-content: flex-start` — so the glyph was **top-anchored, not centred**, and where it landed was `border + padding + halfIcon` against a target of `halfButton`. Those two agree at exactly one breakpoint: `1 + 16 + 24 = 41 = 82/2` at `lg`. Everywhere else they miss by the same 4px, measured **-4.00px at 480-639 (1+12+12=25 vs 29), 640-767 (1+16+12=29 vs 33) and 768-1023 (1+12+20=33 vs 37)** — desktop was centred by coincidence, and the xs-mobile column, which has no label sibling and already used `items-center justify-center`, was correct all along. So the label is now absolutely positioned under the button and the box centres on **both** axes, which makes the glyph centred *by construction* at any button or icon size rather than wherever the arithmetic happens to land. Two things travel with it. The icon's `w-full` becomes `w-6`: `w-full` is a percentage against a shrink-to-fit parent, which is circular, so it was resolving to the glyph's own 24px intrinsic width by fallback — right today, silently wrong the moment that parent gets a definite width. And the label's `mt-2 sm:mt-4` becomes a single `-mt-1`: as a margin between in-flow siblings its real effect was `padding + iconHeight + margin - spanHeight`, drifting the tooltip's bottom edge to button **+15 / +19 / +23 / +27px** across the four breakpoints; `-mt-1` tucks the label's own `py-1` back over the button's 1px border so the TEXT starts level with the bottom of the circle, one number instead of four, and deliberately the tight one — the ring's lowest button already sits near the foot of a short viewport. `pointer-events-none` on the label stops a wide tooltip ("Qualifications") from enlarging the anchor's hover target and pausing the ring off-button. Button geometry is untouched (58/66/74/82px at the four bands, before and after), so the [#93](https://github.com/MA1002643/theabdullahfolio/issues/93) orbit measurements still stand — re-checked, and all of that issue's acceptance still passes. **Verified end to end: 1920 glyph checks — 30 viewport widths (every breakpoint edge, both sides of it, plus real device widths from 320 to 2560) x 2 heights x {normal, reduced-motion} x {idle, hovered}, all 8 buttons each — every glyph centred to 0.000px on both axes in the orbital layout and 0.008px in the xs-mobile column, with each button confirmed square first.** Tooltips separately hovered one button at a time via live coordinates (the ring rotates, so a stale locator misses) across 7 viewports: 8/8 appear and are horizontally centred everywhere except the pre-existing 480-639px band, where buttons overlap each other by 97% so a hover lands on a neighbour — measured on unmodified `main` too, and marginally BETTER after this change (6/8 against 5/8). Tooltip viewport overflow is also better or equal at every size measured, notably 1440x900 going from 3.3px outside the viewport to 0.5px inside; the one remaining 0.6px at 768x700 clips transparent padding, not text. ESLint clean |

#### Orbital nav painted behind the laptop at every width, and orbited a tablet ellipse tuned for a laptop half the size

| | |
|:--|:--|
| **Ref** | [#93](https://github.com/MA1002643/theabdullahfolio/issues/93) (the issue's stacking diagnosis is exactly right; its geometry diagnosis is inverted, and its proposed constants were measured making the worse case worse — see below) |
| **Files** | `src/components/navigation/index.jsx` |
| **Details** | **Two defects, one of them site-wide rather than tablet-only.** (1) The orbital wrapper carried `absolute z-0`, and `position` + a `z-index` makes an element its own **stacking context** — so the `z-50` on every `NavButton` inside only ever ordered the buttons against *each other*, while the ring as a whole sat at z-0 against the laptop's `z-20` (`app/page.js`). A real hit test (move a button to the laptop's centre, ask `elementsFromPoint` what is on top) returns **laptop-over-button at 480, 640, 767, 768, 1023, 1024 and 1440px** — the issue's claim that ≥1024px was unaffected does not hold; desktop simply crosses the art less often, so it was never reported. `z-30` clears the laptop, and cannot reach the maintenance header: the hero row is itself a stacking context (`relative z-10`) and a *sibling* of that header (`z-50`) under `<main>`, so the header wins whatever number is used here — verified by hit test, not by reading the numbers. (2) One set of orbital constants covered **640–1023px, but the laptop does not**: `sm:w-[75%]` renders it **576px wide at a 767px viewport** and `md:w-[22rem]` drops it to a flat **352px at 768px**, a 224px discontinuity in the very object being orbited. Against that, the fixed 691x176px ellipse (`radius 160 x 1.8 x 1.2` by `160 x 0.55`) failed three separate ways, all measured by solving the live ellipse out of the DOM transforms and simulating all 360 rotation angles for all 8 buttons against the laptop's **true alpha silhouette** (the art is a 3/4-perspective laptop, so its bounding box is ~39% transparent and a box test condemns positions that are visually clear): it **overflowed the viewport by up to 59px per side below ~757px wide** (37.2% of samples clipped at 641px); it put **adjacent buttons 96.9% on top of one another across the whole 768–1023 band**, caught live in the browser at 54.3% at 1023x1024; and at 767px it left **53.9% of samples over opaque laptop**, which combined with defect (1) is why that width showed only five of eight buttons, one of them sliced by the screen edge. The overlap has a closed form worth recording: at the ellipse's left/right turnaround two neighbours share an *x* and are separated only vertically by **2b·sin(22.5°) = 0.765b**, so they collide whenever **b < 1.31 x button height** — 88 against a 74px button is straight through that floor, while desktop's 204 against 82px is why desktop looks right. **The fix follows the laptop.** The band splits at 768 (`tablet-sm` / `tablet`); the horizontal semi-axis becomes `min(340, 0.445 x width)` so it tracks a laptop that is three quarters of the viewport in the lower band and a constant in the upper; and the vertical semi-axis is now **derived from viewport height**, because the ring is centred below mid-viewport and each button is anchored by its top-left corner, so it is the lowest button's bottom that runs out of room first — a fixed value either clips short windows or stays too flat to clear the laptop on tall ones. There is a sharp threshold there: below b≈200 the orbit is trapped inside the laptop's vertical band, above b≈250 the buttons pass above and below it. The issue's §4.2 proposed *shrinking* the lower band to 241x98, which measurement shows would bury the ring **inside** a 576px laptop; §4.3's `Math.min/max` clamp was also declined, since clamping x flattens the ellipse's turnarounds into straight edges and the buttons would visibly stall there — the horizontal axis is **scaled** instead, which keeps the motion elliptical. Also replaced the `radius x multiplier.x x xSpacing` chain with the two semi-axes it always denoted: three numbers in two files for two degrees of freedom is most of why nothing in the source ever said "691x176px ellipse". **Verified** across 9 widths x 5 viewport heights (2880 angle-samples per frame): every tablet cell now has **zero viewport clipping, zero adjacent overlap**, and opaque-laptop crossing down from 28.9% to **1.4%** at 1023x1024 and 53.9% to **12.8%** at 767x1024 — at or better than the desktop band's own 15%. Mobile (<640px) and desktop (≥1024px) semi-axes are **pixel-identical** to before (264x55, 297x60.5, 425x204), so those layouts are untouched except that their buttons now also paint in front of the laptop, which is defect (1) being fixed everywhere it existed. Header stacking, hover labels (visible, unclipped, in-viewport at 767 and 1023) and `prefers-reduced-motion` resting positions (8 buttons, none offscreen, none overlapping) all re-checked in the browser. The one trade: on a **short** window in the lower band (767x700, where height caps b at 173) crossing rises 53.9% -> 58.9%, accepted because clipping at that width goes 0% -> 0% while 641px goes 37.2% -> 0%, and with (1) fixed a crossing button is now visible and clickable rather than gone. ESLint clean; the only console errors on the route are a pre-existing reduced-motion hydration mismatch in the loader's `EmblemSeal` `<circle>` and `/api/spotify` 502s, both reproduced identically on unmodified `main`. **Superseded in part by the entry above, on the owner's report, and the diagnosis is worth keeping precisely because the remedy was wrong**: `z-30` cured "the ring is behind the laptop" by making it *permanently in front*, which is the same stacking-context flattening seen from the other side — the ring is meant to pass behind the art on the far half of each lap, so depth is now per-button rather than per-ring. The band-split geometry described here (`tablet-sm` / `tablet`, `min(340, 0.445 x width)`, the height-derived vertical semi-axis) is also gone: bands are gone entirely, one fitted ellipse at a fixed tilt replaces all of them, and the mobile/desktop semi-axes are no longer pixel-identical to the values quoted above |

#### The role line's strike animation re-armed on every scene pause, hiding a heading that had already arrived

| | |
|:--|:--|
| **Ref** | — (review finding on [#146](https://github.com/MA1002643/theabdullahfolio/pull/146), against the latent-engraving entry above) |
| **Files** | `src/components/home/HomeRoleLatent.jsx` |
| **Details** | `useSceneGate` pauses a layer by flipping `running` false **without unmounting it** — the element has to survive for the `IntersectionObserver` to ever see it come back. `HomeRoleLatent`'s plate effect lists `running` in its deps, so every pause ran its teardown, and that teardown removed `is-printing` from the `<h2>`. The `struck` flag was local to the effect, so the next resume re-added the class — and `hero-role-strike` opens at **`opacity: 0` and holds it to 76% of 2960ms**. Cost: a heading that had been legible for minutes went blank for **~2.25s** and re-printed itself, on every tab switch and, more often, every time the hero scrolled out of view and back at `threshold: 0`. The removal was written to stop a client-side navigation back to the homepage finding the type already struck, but that case never reached this line: a real unmount destroys the `<h2>` and takes the class with it. So the class now stays, and `struck` is lifted to a ref that lives as long as the heading does — one strike per element, which is what `both` on the animation already assumed. Verified on the running dev server against a faked `visibilitychange`, with the canvas fingerprinted at three points to prove the gate genuinely cycled: the plate loop painted, stopped while hidden (teardown really ran), and painted again on resume, while the heading held `is-printing` throughout and its **minimum opacity over a 2.6s window — longer than the animation's own blank hold — stayed 1.000**. A hand-run of the old sequence on the same page (remove, then re-add) drops it straight to 0.000, which is the regression this replaces. Remount still re-strikes as designed: an SPA hop to `/projects` and back yields a **different** `<h2>` node that arms, dips to 0 and lands at 1. ESLint clean |

#### A lost canvas context left the plate's frame loop turning, doing nothing

| | |
|:--|:--|
| **Ref** | — (review finding on [#146](https://github.com/MA1002643/theabdullahfolio/pull/146)) |
| **Files** | `src/components/home/HomeRoleLatent.jsx` |
| **Details** | `draw` re-armed itself at the top of every frame and only then bailed on `lost`, so a lost 2D context stopped the drawing but not the loop: the callback kept being scheduled for as long as the layer was on screen. Harmless when the context comes straight back, which is the usual case — but `contextrestored` is listened for precisely because it sometimes does not, and a context that never restores left the main thread being woken for nothing indefinitely. Measured by counting the frames the plate specifically schedules, picked out of the page's own rAF traffic by the callback's source: **60/s while running, 60/s while lost** — a quarter of the page's 240/s, spent entirely on an early return. Now cancelled in `onLost` and started again by hand in `onRestored`, with `draw` also declining to re-arm if a frame was already in flight when the context went, so the cancel cannot be raced. `!R` deliberately keeps scheduling, since that bail only spans a rebuild and the loop has to be there when the plate lands. The restart goes through a `start` indirection that begins as a no-op: both handlers are attached **before** the animated path is built, and the reduced-motion path returns without ever building it, so a restore there repaints through `refresh` and correctly starts no loop. `first` resets with the restart, so a resumed frame measures `dt` from itself rather than from whenever the last frame before the loss landed. Verified across a dispatched loss and restore: plate frames go **60 → 0 → 60**, page-wide rAF drops 240 → 180 by exactly the plate's share, and the plate repaints (28,554 inked). The same probe against the previous code reads 60 → **60** → 60, so it was measuring the right thing. Reduced-motion still frame re-checked and unchanged: 80.2% inked, no NaN, bit-identical after 2.5s, no page errors. ESLint clean |

#### A `fonts.ready` callback from a torn-down run could blank the live plate

| | |
|:--|:--|
| **Ref** | — (review finding on [#146](https://github.com/MA1002643/theabdullahfolio/pull/146)) |
| **Files** | `src/components/home/HomeRoleLatent.jsx` |
| **Details** | The plate effect re-runs on every pause, resume, and pointer-capability change, and each run registers `document.fonts.ready.then(refresh)` — a promise, which unlike every listener and timer this effect owns has **no unsubscribe**. So a `.then` registered by a run that has since been torn down still fires, and it lands on the live canvas, because this layer pauses *without* unmounting and the element is shared between runs. `rebuild` reassigns `canvas.width`, which clears the bitmap, then rewrites the inline geometry from measurements taken for a run that no longer owns the plate. Reproduced rather than argued: holding `document.fonts.ready` pending behind a gate installed before app code runs, letting the layer mount and print, tearing the run down with no successor, then releasing the gate — the stale callback **blanked the plate from 28,720 inked pixels to 0**, and nothing repaints it while paused, so it stays blank until the next resume. Fixed with a `disposed` flag set in `teardown` and read at the top of `refresh` — the single door every rebuild comes through, so it also covers any future async caller. Verified in the same harness with the guard in place: the stale callback leaves bitmap, geometry and all 28,778 inked pixels untouched, while a hand-run `canvas.width = canvas.width` still zeroes it, proving the probe stays sensitive. Live refreshes are unaffected — resizing 1440→700→1440 re-cuts the plate each time (770×185 → 617×140 → 770×185, inked throughout). Two of the three paths named in review were already safe and are left alone: `contextrestored` is removed from the canvas in `teardown`, and the coalescing timer is cleared there. ESLint clean |

#### The reduced-motion still frame locked two of its three inks and left the third where the plate's size happened to put it

| | |
|:--|:--|
| **Ref** | — (review finding on [#146](https://github.com/MA1002643/theabdullahfolio/pull/146)) |
| **Files** | `src/components/home/homeRoleEngraving.js`, `src/components/home/HomeRoleLatent.jsx` |
| **Details** | `alignAtCentre` exists so the one frame a `prefers-reduced-motion` reader gets is the frame where the latent word is at maximum contrast, and it did that by measuring separation 1's beat against the reference and returning the phase that cancels it. `paintStill` then applied that single number to separations **1 and 2**. Each ink is cut at its own `REST_TILT` and `REST_PITCH`, so each arrives at the plate's centre with its own beat: the shared lock zeroed ink 1 exactly and moved ink 2 to an arbitrary place. Measured on the real module rather than a model of it — the residual is **0.75 of a cycle on a 720×300 plate, 0.54 on 430×190, 0.12 on 1180×470**: near the worst case, near the worst case again, and near the best, entirely by accident of geometry. The cost lands on the **ground**, not the word: three inks stacked on one line print a cleaner field than three spread across three, while the word's own light barely moves, so what the residual takes is the contrast the word is read against. Fixed by returning one lock per separation, index-aligned with `seps` and zero for the reference, with the caller applying `locks[i]`. Word-to-ground luminance at the plate centre rises **1.0–3.7%** across five geometries (720×300, 1180×470, 430×190, 900×360, 512×220) and every separation's residual beat goes to zero to six decimals. Verified end-to-end in Chrome under CDP-emulated `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce`, since that is the only path that reaches this code: the plate paints 80.2% inked at mean alpha 15.2 with **no NaN pixels** — the failure an undefined lock would have produced — and is bit-identical after 2.5s, which is the still frame's own contract. Sole caller; no other consumer of the changed return type. ESLint clean |

#### The homepage glow layer's "no ambient video ships for this route" claim outlived the video's restoration

| | |
|:--|:--|
| **Ref** | — (review finding on [#146](https://github.com/MA1002643/theabdullahfolio/pull/146)) |
| **Files** | `README.md` |
| **Details** | The "Homepage causeway scene" row claimed **"Procedural only — no ambient video ships for this route"** and rested an LCP argument on it, while the two rows immediately below it document an ambient lake loop and filmed lantern flames on that same route. Checked against what renders rather than against the prose, because the water row describes itself as *restored after being withdrawn* and a withdrawn layer would have made the claim true: `page.js` mounts `HomeSceneWater`, which renders `HomeSceneVideo` on the primary path and only swaps to the `HomeSceneLivePlate` WebGL warp when the clip fails, and both rungs it sources — `causeway-720.mp4` and `causeway-1080.mp4` — are present in `public/background/`. So the route does ship ambient video and the claim was false, not merely loose. It was also load-bearing in the wrong direction: it is the premise the flames row explicitly overturns ("the premise was right and the conclusion wrong, because this route was **already** shipping a clip for its lake"), so a reader budgeting assets from the glow row would have reached the conclusion the next row exists to correct. Narrowed to what is actually true and actually `HomeSceneGlow`'s to claim — that **this layer** is procedural and zero-byte, which is what earns a glow canvas its place on an LCP-critical page — and pointed at the two rows that own the video's cost and its fallback chain, rather than restating either. Documentation-only; the fallback pointer was checked against `HomeSceneWater.jsx`, whose video-or-warp-never-both behaviour the flames row already describes |

#### Both restatements of the iris ramp's floor were wrong, each in a different half

| | |
|:--|:--|
| **Ref** | — (review findings on [#146](https://github.com/MA1002643/theabdullahfolio/pull/146), against the latent-engraving entry above) |
| **Files** | `src/app/globals.css`, `README.md` |

The comment on the iris gradient in `globals.css` named the right kind of claim about the **wrong stop** ("the orchid stop is the floor of the ramp and the one to check first if these are ever retuned"); the README palette table named the **right stop with the wrong number** ("floor stop is periwinkle at 5.74:1" — 5.74 is orchid's desktop figure). The source of truth, the contrast block in `globals.css`, was correct throughout: recomputing every stop's relative luminance against the backdrops it documents (0.0733 desktop / 0.0751 phone) reproduces it exactly — aqua 5.98/5.89, orchid 5.74/5.66, rose 5.72/5.64, periwinkle **5.60/5.52** — as does the README's own "between 5.52:1 and 5.98:1" further down. Left alone, the CSS comment aimed the next retune at the stop with the *third* most headroom and away from the one with the least, and the README understated the floor's tightness by 0.14.
